# Beta battle plan · v0.9

Status: running (B1 waits for B0; B2 done; B3–B5 atomised, partly done; B6 planned in `plans/2026-10-27-tiling.md`; B7 and B8 atomised from the D82 picks; Q1–Q3, Q5–Q20 open, architect defaults apply until answered) · Repo: `navo` · Branch: `beta-bN-<atom>` per atom · Started: 2026-10-27

## Goal

Navo becomes safe, configurable and pleasant for daily work on Windows. A mistaken Shift+Del or overwrite can be undone for days. The window works at any width. Automotive and embedded files open in calm, rich viewers. Workspaces, tiled panes, find files and a preview pane make daily work faster. Modules, themes and keys become the user's choice. Each milestone below ships on its own as a GitHub pre-release.

Sources: roadmap "Right after the alpha" and "v1 Beta"; D13, D14, D16, D21, D58, D65, D68, D72–D82; P38 A5/A6; P43; `docs/retention.md` and the plans on `backend/beta-retention-on-main`.

## What exists today (checked at `6873ea06` and `backend/beta-retention-on-main` at `670250a5`)

- **Retention engine, not yet on main.** `navo-retention` (capture, restore, purge, reconcile, adopt, bounded maintenance), `navo-store::RetentionCatalog` (schema V7, `list_after` paging) and `[retention]` settings (`retain-deletes`, `retain-overwrites`, `days`, `max-volume-mib`, `min-free-mib`; both flags default `false`). Everything runs only with `--experimental-retention` (`navo-app/src/cli.rs:80`, `gui.rs:338-346`). Missing for users: activation, settings UI, Recently deleted, Ctrl+Z restore, periodic maintenance, terminal metadata pruning, native Windows runtime checks. Containers still use `.navo-retained-*` names.
- **Undo** exists for rename jobs only: `UndoOffer` (`navo-ui/src/browser/rename_batch/hooks.rs:10`) and `window_view/undo.rs`.
- **Fonts.** `navo-ui/fonts/` bundles Archivo and JetBrains Mono. `tokens/design_b.rs` sets `ui_font` and `numbers_font` to Archivo. Inter and Space Grotesk are not bundled. The `appearance.font` and `appearance.font-size` settings exist (default Inter, 13) but nothing applies them.
- **Layout.** Two panes in one `Splitter` (`window_view/layout.rs`). No minimum window size (`title_bar/mod.rs:37`). Header and row cells compute widths in separate code (`file_list/header.rs`, `file_list/row.rs`). `ListParts` hides columns only by user choice. Tabs never overflow (`primitives/tabs.rs`). `test_window::open_sized` already opens a test window at a given width.
- **Settings sheet** is hand-written: `Section` and `Field` enums and four `FieldSpec` tables (`navo-ui/src/settings/fields.rs`). No module schemas. `navo-plugins` is an empty crate.
- **Shortcuts.** A user keymap with live reload and a Shortcuts page exist (`window_view/user_keymap.rs`, `settings_sheet/shortcuts.rs`, P20 done).
- **Automotive.** DBC and A2L files open in F3 as one flat table of description rows (`navo-ui/src/window_view/viewer/kinds/description.rs:115-144`, checked at `c016ee35`); D82 replaces it with an outline and an inspector (B3 V7a–V7k). Rich ELF/MAP is merged; CSV and CAN trace column resizing (P38 A5/A6) is not.
- **Background work.** P43 is written and its Q1–Q3 were accepted; only J0 ran. `IoQueue` never classifies a volume.
- **Not present:** workspaces, refresh mode per location, module registry, safe mode, ignore list, copy rules, custom colour or icon themes, soak test, spreadsheet viewer, NSIS installer.

## Before B1 starts (B0, not a release)

These are running now and are not atomised here. B1 starts when all three are true.

1. `backend/beta-retention-on-main` is merged to `main`, with the Navo names (`.navo-retained-*`, `navo.db`) and a Navo migration of `.navo-retained-*` containers.
2. Record performance (`feat/perf-recording`) is merged, so each milestone can attach a performance zip from a Windows PC.
3. The orchestrator fills the `Read` line ranges of the B1 atoms from the merged `main`. File names below are from the retention branch.

## Decisions

Final for this plan. A worker that finds one wrong stops and reports.

- D1 — **Milestone = pre-release.** Each milestone merges as squash commits, passes the full gate and Windows CI, and the `deployer` publishes a pre-release `beta-<date>` with the exe and zip. The owner checks it on a Windows PC before the next milestone's last atom merges.
- D2 — **Retention is on for everyone.** The `--experimental-retention` flag goes away. Coordinated mode (mutation leases, private-path protection) is the only mode. `--no-retention` starts without leases and without retaining, as a safe-mode escape; it keeps the reserved-folder protection (retention plan D4). One-instance requests must match, as today.
- D3 — **Volumes that cannot retain fall back openly.** When the target provider has no atomic rename or no file identity (network shares, some USB file systems), Shift+Del shows the ordinary permanent-delete confirmation with one more line: "This drive cannot keep deleted files." It never refuses and never retains silently.
- D4 — **Recently deleted is a side sheet** on the existing `side_sheet` (like Settings), opened by the command `files.recently-deleted` (Ctrl+K and the File menu). It lists retained entries newest first. Restore returns an entry to its original path; on a collision it keeps both with " (restored)" added. "Delete now" purges one entry after a confirmation. Its visual direction comes from designer round R1.
- D5 — **Ctrl+Z after a retained delete or overwrite restores it.** `UndoOffer` gains a kind: `Rename` (today) or `Retained(Vec<RetentionId>)`. The undo confirmation names the count and the folder, as rename undo does.
- D6 — **Maintenance runs on a timer, too.** Bounded maintenance runs once after start-up recovery and then every 6 hours, on a worker, holding the mutation lease, at most 32 purges per pass (the existing per-job bound).
- D7 — **Fonts (D78).** Inter for the interface and numbers (tabular numerals), JetBrains Mono for paths, viewers and hex, Space Grotesk SemiBold for the wordmark, window titles and sheet titles. Archivo is removed. Font files come from the official releases (Inter 4.1 `rsms/inter`, Space Grotesk 2.0.0 `floriankarsten/space-grotesk`), with their OFL licence files beside them. The `appearance.font` and `appearance.font-size` settings apply live.
- D8 — **Responsive layout (D80 1–6).** Minimum window size 480 × 360 px. Header and row cells take their widths from one function. Names end in an ellipsis; Ext, Size and Date are fixed width. Columns drop automatically in the order Ext, Date, Size when the pane is too narrow; this never changes the user's View setting. Below 900 px window width the window shows one pane (Q3). Tabs shrink to a minimum width, then the rest move into a "+N" overflow menu. Layout tests render at 480, 800 and 1280 px and run in the normal nextest step of the gate.
- D9 — **Tiling panes follow their own plan.** The owner picked direction D plus free layouts, user presets and Shift+drag swap (D82). `plans/2026-10-27-tiling.md` holds its decisions (TD1–TD14) and atoms (TL1–TL17).
- D10 — **Models (owner, 2026-10-26).** The main workforce is Codex Sol 6.1 through crew: api-builder and ui-builder at medium, high where the table says so; checker on Sol 6.1 high. Opus 5.5 medium for senior, designer and reviewer-senior. Sonnet 5.5 medium only for trivial edits (patcher) and the documenter. Haiku only for scout lookups. Never Opus high. The B1 and B2 rows were written before this rule; B1 atoms run on the new models. *Amended 2026-10-29:* Codex Sol 6.1 high is the standard Codex worker; DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (through dshw) may run pure-backend atoms (core, fs, store, index, app back ends, xtask), never an atom that touches `navo-ui`; Sonnet for light and UI work; Opus 5.5 medium for hard atoms.
- D12 — **Order (D81).** The owner's priority items come first, grouped as B3–B6. The items moved into the beta from v1.0 and later follow as B7–B9. Every other beta item comes after them (B10–B16), including background work and Raw/Pretty, which the roadmap listed "right after the alpha".
- D13 — **Open a terminal here is a core command now.** `files.open_terminal` starts the configured terminal in the active pane's folder. It moves behind the module registry when B12 lands. Network and archive folders refuse with a status line.
- D14 — **Workspaces belong to a window (Q9), shown as a tray (D82).** A window holds an ordered list of one to nine named workspaces; Alt+1…9 reach them by position. Each holds the layout with its ratios (tiling TD13), panes, tabs, history and the target pane, not listings. Alt+N makes a new one from the current layout. Switching keeps the workspace you leave exactly as you left it; its unsaved changes stay a draft until Ctrl+S saves them or Discard drops them (Q12). Saved states and drafts are kept in the session file. *Changed on 2026-10-27 with D82:* the first D14 had nine fixed slots, where an empty slot copied the layout and switching saved; the tray prototype the owner picked shows a list that grows with Alt+N and drafts.
- D15 — **Accessibility basics, scope.** Narrator reads the file list rows, dialogs and Ctrl+K results with a role and a name. Every focusable element shows a focus ring. Every animation honours reduced motion. Windows contrast themes switch Navo to the system colours. A UI scale setting (80–200 %) applies live.
- D16 — **DBC and A2L in F3: outline and inspector (D82, direction B).** The Rich view shows an outline on the left and an inspector on the right; Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I toggle them (D58). DBC outline: sender nodes → messages (ID, name, cycle) → signals (start|length, unit); messages without a known sender sit under "No sender". A2L outline: a module line, Characteristics grouped by kind (MAP, CURVE, VAL_BLK, VALUE, others), Measurements, Compu methods. The inspector shows the selected item: path crumbs, kind and name, problems, fact tiles; for DBC the bit layout, formula, receivers, value table and sibling signals (a selected message shows its bit layout and its signal list: the "signals in the middle" of D82, Q14); for A2L the limits, axes and compu method with "also used by", or a compu method's coefficients and users. Keys: / or Ctrl+F find, N and Shift+N next and previous hit, J/K or arrows move, Left/Right fold, B bookmark, Esc clears the find. Bookmarks save per file by source line. The flat table goes away.
- D17 — **Workspace tray (D82, direction D).** The status bar ends in a workspace button (grid icon, name, "2/4", "Draft · n"); clicking it pins the tray. Holding Alt in the file list shows the tray after the modifier-hints delay, above the hints drawer; Alt+n shows it at once and previews workspace n with the ribbon "Previewing <name> · release Alt to switch · Esc to stay"; releasing Alt switches. Each card shows a small layout preview with folder names, the number, the name, "open" for the current one, and Save and Discard when it has a draft, else rename, move left and right, and delete. Alt+N makes a new workspace. Alt+Shift+Left/Right reorder, because Alt+Left/Right stay Back and Forward (the prototype used Alt+Left/Right). Delete asks nothing; Ctrl+Z restores. The tray never appears inside a viewer, a dialog or Ctrl+K.
- D18 — **Inspector column (D82, direction A).** Ctrl+Q shows or hides a column right of the panes: preview on top, properties below. Ctrl+I folds the properties part; Alt+Enter moves focus into it. Width 360 px by default, 280–560 px by dragging, saved in settings. It follows the active pane's cursor and reuses the preview loaders and pools. Below 1200 px window width, Ctrl+Q opens today's preview side sheet instead (Q15). Ctrl+Shift+T switches the active pane to thumbnail rows. Properties: General, Dates, Attributes, Owner and permissions (read only), Hashes on demand (CRC32, MD5, SHA-256 with Copy). Edits (Q16) apply with Apply or Ctrl+Enter as one job with Ctrl+Z; Revert drops them; marked files show mixed values that stay untouched unless changed.
- D19 — **Find files in Ctrl+K (D82, direction A).** Ctrl+K gets four modes: Names (today), Content, Commands, Collections. Alt+F7 opens it in Content mode with the active pane's folder as scope. Words are the text; `ext:`, `size:`, `mod:`, `attr:`, `in:`, `arc:` and `re:` tokens are filters shown as chips; Alt+C toggles case, Alt+R regex. The palette previews the first hits; Enter opens a hit in F3 at its line. Ctrl+Enter in Content mode sends the search into a new tab of the active pane as a virtual folder (in Names mode Ctrl+Enter keeps "other pane"). The results tab streams hits with progress; Esc stops and keeps what was found. File rows carry their hit lines; F3, F5, F6, Space, F2 (rename) and drag act on the real files; copying asks whether to keep the folder structure. "Keep as collection" saves a saved search or a snapshot; "Add to collection" and Alt+Insert add files by hand; collections open as tabs with a From column. Details in B7 D19a–D19c.
- D11 — **Checks.** Workers run `cargo xtask check -p <crate>` and the nextest filter in their row. The full `cargo xtask gate` runs once per milestone on the merge branch. One cargo build at a time (nice 10, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=8`). Windows CI is the real gate (later also a Windows VM test agent); a Linux run is a quick filter.
- D20 — **Libraries (D88, 2026-10-29).** A library comes in only with its feature, pinned, after a short test or measurement shows it helps. GPL, AGPL and LGPL crates are never added. The agreed list is the library map in `plans/2026-10-28-speed.md`.

B1 and B2 run in parallel: they share no component. B3, B4 and B5 may run in parallel with B1: they share no component with it. Inside each milestone, atoms with no shared file and no `After` link run in parallel, but only one cargo gate runs at a time (D11).

## BI · Index like Everything

D83: built before the other beta work. The service already reads the MFT and follows the USN journal, and it already saves a snapshot. The owner still saw two faults on a Windows PC (alpha-2026-10-27c): the app walked `C:\Users\…` again and again, and the service used about 235 MB. The walks came from the fallback when the service did not answer in time (fixed in `ef34edd6`: a silent service no longer starts a walk). The open question is why the service was silent at all.

### Components

- `navo-index` host, NTFS index and pipe server: answers, start-up and memory.
- `navo-app` search hooks: which roots the app ever walks.
- `navo-app` bench: the comparison command.

### Atoms

| # | Atom | Role | Effort | After | Check | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IX1 | Find why the pipe goes silent. Measure, with tests: time from service start to first answer (snapshot present, snapshot stale, journal reset), how long the table write lock is held during the first MFT read, USN batches and saves, and pipe answer time while each runs. Write failing tests for every case over 50 ms. | checker | high | — | the failing tests, with numbers in the report | done |
| IX2 | Answer while busy. Queries and status read a published, read-only copy of the table (swapped in after each batch), never the lock a writer holds. Status always answers within 50 ms, with a "building" or "updating" state. | senior | medium | IX1 | IX1 tests pass; `cargo test -p navo-index` | todo |
| IX3 | Start from the saved index. On start the service loads the snapshot and answers from it first, then catches up from the journal; a full MFT read only when the journal id or volume changed. Save on stop and after big batches, not only every 30 s. | senior | medium | IX2 | restart test: first answer under 1 s with a 1 M-entry snapshot | done |
| IX4 | No walks on NTFS. When the service is installed, the app walks only network and non-NTFS roots, never a drive or folder the service covers, whatever the service answers. Busy note stays as is. | api-builder (Sol medium) | medium | IX2 | roots tests; a test that a silent service starts no walk of a covered root | todo |
| IX5 | Memory per part. The service reports its memory by part (name table, file-id map, journal buffers, pipe sessions, results) in its status and the perf zip, reusing the heap counter from the perf work. | api-builder (Sol medium) | medium | perf memory merged | unit tests for the report | done |
| IX6 | Shrink the biggest parts IX5 shows. Target: service under 100 MB for 1 M files. Keep the 50 bytes per entry table budget. | senior | medium | IX5, owner perf zip | budget tests | IX6a done (lock budgets); memory part ready |
| IX7 | Compare with Everything. `navo --bench index` prints cold start, warm start, first answer, query time for 5 fixed queries and memory; the owner runs it next to Everything on a Windows PC. | api-builder (Sol medium) | medium | IX3 | command runs on Windows CI against a temp volume | done |
| IX8 | Keep the service in step with the app. The installer and the updater update the index service when its version changes (one UAC prompt then, none otherwise); the app warns when the running service is older than itself. Found on 2026-10-30: the per-user Velopack install left the old admin-installed service in `C:\Program Files\Navo\Index` running. | senior | medium | — | service version tests; VM install over an older service | IX8a–IX8f and IX8h–IX8p shipped; IX8g dropped; IX8q–IX8t, IX8u and IX8v1a–IX8v4b open (2026-11-04) |
| IX8a | Update core: new crate `navo-update` with `InstallKind`, `Release`, `FeedError`, the `UpdateFeed` trait and the version rule (newer only; equal and older refused). No Velopack, no network. Brief in "IX8 · Updates in place" below. | api-builder · DeepSeek V4.1 Flash or Sol 6.1 high | medium | — | `cargo nextest run -p navo-update version` | done (shipped) |
| IX8b | Update state machine: `step(state, event, now) -> (state, effects)` for the start-up check, Check now, found, nothing new, progress, ready and every failure (offline, rate-limited with a 1 h wait, bad feed, interrupted, damaged, busy); portable and unpackaged copies emit nothing. | api-builder · DeepSeek V4.1 Flash or Sol 6.1 high | high | IX8a | `cargo nextest run -p navo-update machine` | done (shipped) |
| IX8c | Update runner: one worker thread drives the machine through an `UpdateFeed`; one request at a time; a check gives up after 30 s and drops the late answer; a snapshot for the UI; tests with a scripted fake feed. | api-builder · Sol 6.1 high | high | IX8b | `cargo nextest run -p navo-update runner` | done (shipped) |
| IX8d | Velopack feed: `VelopackFeed` in `navo-app` over `UpdateManager` + `GithubSource` (`https://github.com/trykA123/navo`, pre-releases on, no token, no downgrade); detects the install kind; maps Velopack errors to `FeedError`. | api-builder · Sol 6.1 high | high | IX8a | `cargo nextest run -p navo-app updater::feed` | done (shipped) |
| IX8e | Apply at the next start: Velopack's start-up auto-apply off; a start that opens a window applies a staged package before any window, job or recovery runs, and only when no other Navo process from this install runs; otherwise it waits. | senior · Opus 5.5 | medium | IX8a | `cargo nextest run -p navo-app updater::apply`; VM: one process applies and restarts with the same arguments; a second profile running blocks it | done (shipped) |

### IX8 · Updates in place (in-app updater)

Atomised 2026-11-02 (architect Elendil). The status column holds the state of 2026-11-04. D22a–D22i are proposed; when accepted, `docs/decisions.md` gets one entry for them.

**What the user gets.** An installed Navo finds a new release by itself, downloads it quietly and becomes the new version the next time it starts. Settings → Help shows the state and has Check now. The index service follows in the same restart, with one UAC prompt only when the service itself changed. A portable copy never updates itself.

**What exists (checked at `0406552d`, paths under `crates/navo-app/src/`).**

- `velopack` =1.2.161 is already a dependency. `installer.rs:18-25` runs `VelopackApp::build()` with only the uninstall hook. Velopack's start-up auto-apply is on by default (`velopack-1.2.161/src/app.rs:30-56`): once a package is staged, every start applies it, also a second launch that only forwards to a running Navo.
- `release.yml:132-175` packs with `vpk` 1.2.161: pack id `NavoApp`, channel `win`, version `0.0.1-<alpha|beta>.<date><letter>`. Every release is a GitHub pre-release. Each carries `releases.win.json` with one Full package, its size, SHA-1 and SHA-256 (checked on `alpha-2026-11-02a`: 32.9 MB). No delta packages.
- The binary does not know its release: `navo --version` prints the Cargo version `0.0.1` (`main.rs:146`).
- `--index-service install` (elevated, `index_service/mod.rs:88-161`) copies the running exe to `C:\Program Files\Navo\Index`, re-registers and restarts the service with rollback, and removes the legacy Ferrite service. The pipe protocol has no version (`navo-index/src/pipe/frames.rs:51-110`).
- Settings → Help shows "Navo <version>" (`navo-ui/src/settings/help_page.rs:47-72`). Settings → Search shows the service state with Install and Remove (`navo-ui/src/settings/search_page.rs:13-40`).
- Navo runs one process per profile (`instance/naming.rs:6-15`); the windows of a profile share it.

**Velopack facts** (read in the crate source in the cargo registry, `velopack-1.2.161`). Verified on crates.io 2026-11-02: 1.2.161 is the newest stable version (MIT, 2026-09-29); 1.3.39-g8d2844f (2026-10-17) is a pre-release and stays out. `GithubSource::new(url, token, prerelease)` makes one API call (`api.github.com/repos/<repo>/releases?per_page=10`, 60 per hour per IP without a token), then fetches `releases.win.json` of each release from `github.com`; a feed that does not parse is skipped silently, so a set of bad feeds reads as `RemoteIsEmpty`. `UpdateManager::new` fails with `NotInstalled` without a Velopack manifest; `get_is_portable()` is true for the portable zip. `download_updates` takes a lock, writes `<name>.partial`, checks size and SHA-256 (SHA-1 when the feed has no SHA-256), then renames; the next download deletes old partial files. `HttpOptions.TimeoutMilliseconds` defaults to 0 (no timeout) and `GithubSource` cannot set it. TLS is `ureq` 3.5 with rustls and the bundled `webpki-roots`, not the Windows certificate store. `on_after_update_fast_callback` is killed after 15 s. **Unverified:** whether `Update.exe apply` closes or kills other running processes of the app; whether `ureq` refuses an https → http redirect.

**Appetite.** Four agent days, VM run and review included. Over that: cut IX8n (the ready notice) and IX8g (bump the revision by hand). Never cut the apply guard or the failure tests.

**Non-goals.** Delta packages. Channels or switching between alpha and beta. Rollback from the UI. Updating the portable zip or a bare `navo.exe`. Restarting Navo by itself. Metered-network detection. A signed feed (open question). Linux and macOS.

**Alternatives considered.**

- Velopack's default auto-apply at start: lost. It applies from a forwarding second launch while the first Navo runs.
- Apply at quit (`wait_exit_then_apply_updates` when the last window closes): lost. Quit stops jobs with a 10 s limit (`navo-ui/src/window_view/closing.rs:10`), a Navo kept in the tray rarely quits, and a Windows shutdown can cut the apply. At start nothing runs yet.
- `HttpSource` on `releases/latest/download`: lost. `latest` skips pre-releases, and every Navo release is one.
- An own GitHub client: lost. Velopack already stages, locks and checks size and SHA-256.
- The service update inside Velopack's after-update hook: lost. The hook dies after 15 s; a UAC prompt can wait longer.
- Service version = release version: lost. Every update would ask for UAC.

#### Decisions for IX8 (proposed)

- D22a — **Velopack against GitHub.** `UpdateManager` with `GithubSource::new("https://github.com/trykA123/navo", None, true)` and `UpdateOptions { AllowVersionDowngrade: false, .. }`. `velopack` stays =1.2.161 and `vpk` in `release.yml` stays the same version; they move together. The feed stays as `release.yml` writes it today.
- D22b — **Only an installed copy updates.** `Installed` (from Setup.exe) checks, downloads and applies. `Portable` (Velopack's portable zip) and `Unpackaged` (the bare `navo.exe` asset, a dev build, any `NotInstalled`) make no request, download nothing and apply nothing. Settings → Help says: "This copy does not update itself. Get the installer from GitHub."
- D22c — **When it checks.** With `updates.check` on (default), one check 10 s after the first window shows. Check now in Settings → Help works at any time, also with the setting off. A found release downloads at once in the background. One check or download runs at a time. A check gives up after 30 s.
- D22d — **Apply only at the next start.** `installer.rs` turns Velopack's start-up auto-apply off. A start that opens a window (`Command::Open`) applies a staged newer package before profile choice, instance start, recovery, retention maintenance or any window, and only when no other Navo process runs from this install's folder. Otherwise the package waits for a later start. Navo never restarts by itself. Command-line starts (`--index-service`, `--version`, helpers, Velopack hooks) never apply.
- D22e — **Service revision.** `SERVICE_REVISION: u32` in `navo-index` starts at 1. The service answers `VersionV1` with the revision and the build tag (`NAVO_BUILD`, else `dev`). A service that cannot answer is older. `cargo xtask service-revision` stores a hash of the service sources and fails when they change, until the developer runs `--bump` (users get the new service with one UAC prompt) or `--same`. **Amended 2026-11-04:** IX8g is dropped and no guard exists. The developer bumps `SERVICE_REVISION` by hand in the change that alters the service; the reviewer checks it. This departs from D89 (3), which names an `xtask` guard; the owner amended D89 (3) on 2026-11-04 to "revision bumped by hand; the reviewer checks it" (the orchestrator records it in `docs/decisions.md`).
- D22f — **Service step.** After the first window shows, on a worker: when the app is `Installed`, the Navo Index service runs and its revision is lower than the app's (or unknown), Navo asks once for elevation and runs `--index-service install`. Same or higher revision: nothing. Declined or failed: `<data>/index-service-declined` keeps that revision, Navo does not ask again for it, and Settings → Search shows "Older than Navo" with Update. A portable copy only shows the status. Setup.exe over an older version takes the same path on its first start.
- D22g — **Quiet.** No dialog, badge or toast for updates. Settings → Help shows one line under the version: "Checking for updates…", "Navo is up to date", "Downloading Navo <version> (40 %)", "Navo <version> is ready. It updates when you next start Navo.", or a failure line. When a download finishes, one window shows the ready text once as a status notice. Fade only, about 80 ms.
- D22h — **Failures stay quiet.** Each failure gives one log line with its kind and one line in Settings → Help: offline "Could not reach GitHub"; rate-limited "GitHub asks Navo to wait. Next try after <time>" (1 h, also for Check now); bad feed "The update list could not be read"; interrupted "The download stopped. It starts again next time"; damaged "The download was damaged and was removed"; busy "Another Navo is updating". No dialog, no retry loop.
- D22i — **The service is its own exe (proposed, owner request 2026-11-02).** Today Task Manager shows the service as a second "navo.exe" (`C:\Program Files\Navo\Index\navo.exe --index-service run`), which looks like a stray Navo; a SYSTEM service cannot group under the user's app. The service becomes `navo-index.exe`, built from a new crate `navo-index-service` (library plus `[[bin]] navo-index`), with its own version resource (FileDescription and ProductName "Navo Index", the Navo icon, FileVersion and ProductVersion as the app's) and the service description "Keeps the file index for Navo search up to date." A new crate, not a second `[[bin]]` of `navo-app`: a bin there shares `navo-app`'s dependencies and `build.rs`, so it would carry the "Navo" resource and reach the service code only through `#[path]` tricks; the crate depends on `navo-index`, `navo-store`, `navo-fs`, `navo-core`, `navo-diag` and `windows-sys`, never on `navo-ui` or `gpui`. `navo-app` keeps the client side (query, elevation, install and remove commands) through the library. Install copies the `navo-index.exe` that sits next to `navo.exe`; without it, Install refuses with "navo-index.exe is missing next to navo.exe". The PDF helper stays a child of `navo.exe`.

#### Data safety, security and privacy

- **Feed trust.** The feed is not signed; Navo has no code signing yet (D73, D88). Trust rests on GitHub serving `trykA123/navo` over HTTPS, checked by rustls against the bundled Mozilla roots. Velopack checks each package's size and SHA-256 against the feed before staging it; a mismatch stages nothing and leaves the installed version as it is. The checksum protects the download, not a stolen GitHub account or release token.
- **HTTPS only.** The repository URL is a constant. No setting, file or environment variable changes it in a release build. The folder feed of IX8q exists only in `tester` builds; a test proves the release path never reads its variable. No token is ever sent.
- **No downgrade.** Velopack's downgrade option is off, and `navo-update` refuses an equal or older version by itself. A release removed from GitHub rolls nobody back.
- **No restart while work runs.** Navo never restarts by itself. Apply happens only at a start, before this process has windows, jobs, recovery or maintenance, and never while another Navo process from the same install runs (another profile, a forwarding launch, a helper). The process check compares image paths under the install folder and skips the own process id; a failed check counts as "another runs".
- **Interrupted downloads.** Velopack writes `.partial` and renames it only after the checksum passes; the next download removes old partial files. A killed download never leaves a package that applies.
- **Elevation.** One UAC prompt, only for an older running service and an installed app, at most once per revision. Never inside a Velopack hook, never for a portable copy, never to replace a newer service with an older one. Known gap, also true today: the elevated install copies the service exe (`navo-index.exe` after IX8v3b) from the per-user install folder, which the user can write; code signing would let the elevated step check the publisher.
- **Privacy.** An automatic check sends one request to `api.github.com` (the release list) and up to ten to `github.com` (the small `releases.win.json` files). A download fetches one package (about 33 MB). GitHub sees the IP address, the time and the `ureq` user agent. The requests carry no user id, no Navo version, no settings and no paths (`GithubSource` ignores Velopack's staged user id). Logs keep versions and error kinds only. Opt-out: Settings → Help → "Check for updates automatically" off; then Navo sends nothing by itself.
- **Disk.** Velopack keeps the staged package in `%LocalAppData%\NavoApp\packages` and removes older ones after an apply.

#### Risks and rollback

- R1 — A stolen GitHub account or token ships a bad build to every installed copy. Only account security prevents it today.
- R2 — Networks that inspect TLS fail the check with the bundled roots. The user sees "Could not reach GitHub"; nothing else breaks.
- R3 — A check hangs (Velopack has no timeout here). The runner gives up after 30 s and ignores a late answer; the stuck thread ends with the process.
- R4 — The first start after an update is a few seconds slower while Velopack applies.
- Rollback: `updates.check` off stops automatic requests. A bad release is fixed by a newer release (no downgrade). A merged atom is reverted with `git revert <sha>`; IX8e alone only turns auto-apply off, which is safe to keep.

#### IX8b — State machine

- **Read:** IX8a's `lib.rs`; D22b, D22c, D22h.
- **Change:** `src/machine.rs` and tests. `UpdateState { Unsupported(InstallKind), Off, Idle, Checking, UpToDate, Downloading { version, percent }, Ready { version }, Failed { error, retry_after: Option<Instant> } }`. Events: `Started { auto: bool, staged: Option<Version> }`, `CheckNow`, `Checked(Result<Found, FeedError>)`, `Progress(u8)`, `Downloaded(Result<(), FeedError>)`, `AutoChanged(bool)`. Effects: `Check`, `Download(Release)`. Rules: Unsupported never changes and never emits; a staged newer version starts as Ready with no request; Check now while Checking or Downloading does nothing; RateLimited sets `retry_after = now + 1 h` and refuses every check until then; equal or older is UpToDate; `Downloaded(Err)` is Failed, never Ready; time comes in as an argument.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-update machine`: one test per rule, plus offline at start, bad feed, interrupted download, portable and unpackaged emit nothing for every event, auto off emits nothing at start while Check now still checks.

Each feed call carries a generation; a check that has not returned after 30 s becomes `Failed(Offline)` and its late answer is dropped. `stop()` returns within 1 s when idle and does not wait for a download. A `FakeFeed` behind a `test-support` feature (IX8o uses it).
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-update runner`: a hanging check times out; its late answer is ignored; Check now during a download is ignored; progress reaches the snapshot; stop while idle takes under 1 s.

#### IX8u — Own service exe

- **Read:** `crates/navo-app/Cargo.toml`, `crates/navo-app/build.rs` (resource script), `crates/navo-app/src/main.rs:84-100` (the `IndexService` early return), `index_service/mod.rs`, `index_service/memory.rs:1-20` (`crate::diag::probe`), the users of `index_service::` (`installer.rs`, `cli.rs`, `bench/index.rs`, `search_hooks/service.rs`, `search_hooks/index/presence.rs`, `search_hooks/index/roots.rs`), `.github/workflows/release.yml:95-170`.

Tester builds: `target/<profile>/navo-index.exe` sits next to `navo.exe`, so Install works the same.

The CLI parse tests move to `navo-app`; moved tests get their paths fixed (`totals_tests.rs:2` uses `crate::index_service::`).
  - Probe: the Windows memory implementation (`windows_memory.rs`, used by `diag.rs:262` and `metrics/windows.rs:40`) moves to `navo-diag`. It exports `probe()`, total RAM and peak working set; `heartbeat.rs:17` and `memory.rs:11` call `navo_diag::probe`. App callers follow. Unsafe code is allowed only in the Windows FFI module.
  - Resources: one parameterised resource generator shared by both `build.rs` files (service name, file name, icon). FileVersion is the Cargo version; ProductVersion adds `NAVO_BUILD`. Both scripts track their included files and the environment they read.

#### IX8v — Switch and migrate (IX8v1a–IX8v4b)

Rewritten 2026-11-04 after two Terra xhigh plan checks (architect Turgon). Lines below are on main `71d87ce2`, under `crates/navo-app/src/index_service/` unless named. After IX8u these files sit under `crates/navo-index-service/src/`; `version_step.rs` stays in `navo-app`.

**What exists.**

- Install runs a step list with rollback: `install_plan.rs:267-309` and `recover` at `install_plan.rs:311-352`. For an existing service it copies, renames the old image to `.old` and replaces it while the service still runs, and stops only then (`install_plan.rs:270-279`). The configuration and the running state are captured before any step (`installation/windows.rs:100-104`).
- Verify checks the image hash against the running exe, the SCM command, the process image and its creation time (`installation/verify.rs:35-57`, `install_check.rs:47-80`). It opens the process, reads it and drops the handle (`verify.rs:86-113`). It never asks the pipe.
- The copy source is the running exe (`installation/windows.rs:109`). The image is `navo.exe` (`install_plan.rs:12-16`). The command is `"…\navo.exe" --index-service run` (`installation/windows.rs:167-172`).
- The Ferrite upgrade waits only for a pipe connect (`installation/windows.rs:53-55`, `upgrade.rs:77-94`).
- A failed install over an existing service keeps an image it placed new: `RemoveFresh` runs only for a new service (`install_plan.rs:331`).
- `stop` returns at `SERVICE_STOPPED`, which can come before the process has exited, and waits up to 60 s (`installation/windows.rs:343-371`). Remove then deletes only `navo.exe`, five tries 200 ms apart (`installation/windows.rs:303-320`, `removal.rs:14-22`). This fits the "Access is denied" of 2026-11-02.
- Exit code 3 means a verification mismatch only; a timeout is 4, a denial 5, anything else 1 (`elevation_result.rs:13-27`).
- The uninstall callback gives the elevated remove 25 s and then reports "no answer" (`installer.rs:17`, `installer.rs:61-64`). Today's stop alone may take 60 s.
- The pipe client accepts only a server owned by LocalSystem (`navo-index/src/pipe/windows/security.rs:18-38`). One connection per exchange (`pipe/status.rs:36-52`). `SERVICE_REVISION` is 2 (`navo-index/src/pipe/version.rs:7`).
- The automatic prompt needs an installed app and a running service that answers older (`version_step.rs:28-45`). Nothing stops two Navo processes from prompting at once (`version_step.rs:89-116`). Success accepts a missing answer (`version_step.rs:47-50`).
- Settings offers Install only for Not installed and Outdated (`navo-ui/src/settings/search_page.rs:36-38`). Outdated needs an older answer from a running service (`search_hooks/service.rs:49-66`). Settings knows only Done and Failed (`navo-ui/src/window_view/search_hooks.rs:19-22`).
- The app already opens the service with `SERVICE_QUERY_CONFIG` without elevation (`scm/windows.rs:54-59`).

**Decisions for IX8v (2026-11-04).**

- **Install order.** One elevated `--index-service install` does it all. Refuse at once when `navo-index.exe` is missing next to `navo.exe` (D22i text). Capture the configuration and the running state. Copy the sibling to `Index\navo-index.exe.<pid>.tmp`. For an existing service: stop it and wait for its process to end. Then rename an existing `Index\navo-index.exe` to `.old` (the backup), rename the copy into place, back up `service.json`, register `"…\Index\navo-index.exe" run` with the description "Keeps the file index for Navo search up to date." and the recovery actions, start, verify, answer. Then the commit point.
- **Verify and Answer, bound to one process.** After the start, read the SCM process id, open that process with `SYNCHRONIZE | PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION` and keep the handle until the commit point. Through that handle: the image is `Index\navo-index.exe` and has the size and SHA-256 of the sibling; the creation time is after the start. The SCM command is the new one. Then poll the pipe for up to 30 s. Each try reads the answer and the server process id on the same connection. The answer must be `Known { revision: SERVICE_REVISION }`; the server id must equal the retained process's id; the retained process must still run; the SCM must still report the same id. Any miss is a verification mismatch (exit 3). The Ferrite upgrade (`AwaitNew`) uses the same Answer.
- **Commit point.** Nothing touches `Index\navo.exe` before Verify and Answer pass. After them, `DeleteOld` removes the `.old` backup and numbered `.tmp` copies; `DeleteLegacy` deletes `Index\navo.exe` and its numbered `.old` and `.tmp` copies. A refused legacy delete falls back to the reboot schedule below; the install still exits 0 and prints a warning.
- **Rollback.** Any failure before the commit point runs `recover`: stop the replacement, restore `service.json`, rename the `.old` backup back to `navo-index.exe` or remove an image that was not there before (also for an existing service), restore the captured configuration (command, description, recovery, delayed start), and restart the old service when it ran before. Every rollback failure is appended to the error. A failed start exits nonzero by its kind; only a verification mismatch exits 3.
- **Removal, states.** The elevated remove has a 20 s deadline. `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` and the pause states give a valid process id: open the process, check that the SCM still reports that id, send the stop. `START_PENDING`: poll until it runs or stops, then as above. `STOP_PENDING`: open the id when it is not 0; otherwise poll until `STOPPED`. `STOPPED`: the id is not valid; take no handle. After `STOPPED`, wait on a retained handle for at most 5 s. A service that has not stopped within 12 s fails the run with a timeout (exit 4) and nothing is deleted.
- **Removal, files.** Remove deletes `navo-index.exe`, `navo.exe` and their `.old` and `.tmp` copies; Ferrite removal deletes `ferrite.exe` the same way. A file that still refuses is renamed to `<image>.<pid>.old`; if the rename fails too, the run fails and reports the locked image (exit 7 for `navo-index.exe`, 8 for `navo.exe`, 9 for `ferrite.exe`, mapped to `LockedImage`, not 1), while a numbered leftover may still be scheduled. Scheduling uses `MoveFileExW(…, MOVEFILE_DELAY_UNTIL_REBOOT)`. Folders are scheduled after all their files, and only when no unknown file remains. Unknown files are never deleted; the run lists them and keeps the folder. A failed schedule is a failure (exit 1), never code 6.
- **Removal, outcome.** Exit 0: all gone. Exit 6: the service is deleted and every leftover was scheduled; the run prints the paths. The app maps 6 to a deferred removal: the uninstall callback logs `Removal::Deferred` without a paths count (exit 6 cannot carry it), and Settings shows the deferred notice. Windows confirms only the schedule, not the later delete; the VM check proves the delete.
- **Uninstall budget.** The deadline starts before taking the change lock. The lock wait, stop (up to 12 s), process wait (up to 5 s), deletes and schedules share the remaining 20 s deadline, inside the callback's 25 s. A run past 25 s stays "no answer" in the uninstall log; a test proves that outcome.
- **One change at a time.** Every elevated install and remove exclusively opens `%ProgramFiles%\Navo\change.lock` after the executable directory guard creates and pins its trusted parent. A wrong ACL on an existing `change.lock` is rewritten, not refused, because the owner and folder are checked first. Install waits up to 60 s; remove waits at most its remaining deadline (timeout: exit 4). An install that finds the service current skips replacing it but still cleans Ferrite. In the app, the automatic step and Settings actions keep `Local\\NavoIndexServiceChange`. The automatic step only tries it: held means another Navo is on it, so no prompt. After taking it, the step queries again and prompts only when the service is still older. So concurrent starts give at most one prompt.
- **Upgrade path, automatic.** The rule of `version_step.rs:28-45` stays. Revision 2 → 3 prompts once only for an installed app with a running service that answers older. A stopped or silent service, a portable or unpackaged copy, and a revision already declined never prompt.
- **Upgrade path, manual.** Settings → Search shows "Older than Navo" with Update when the service answers older, when the update for this revision was declined or failed, or when the registered command still runs `navo.exe` from `Index` (read with `SERVICE_QUERY_CONFIG`, no elevation), in any state.
- **Success.** The automatic step counts as updated only when the query after the prompt answers `Known { revision >= SERVICE_REVISION }`. No answer counts as failed: the declined marker is written and Settings offers Update. The manual Update succeeds only on exit code 0, which Answer already backs.
- **Revision (owner, 2026-11-04).** IX8v3b bumps `SERVICE_REVISION` from 2 to 3 by hand. IX8g is dropped. D89 (3) is amended to "revision bumped by hand; the reviewer checks it"; the orchestrator writes it into `docs/decisions.md`.
- **Later revisions (owner, 2026-11-04).** A stopped or silent `navo-index.exe` service in a later revision (3 → 4) gets no Update offer for now: only the old image name marks a service older without an answer. Revisit after IX12.
- **VM path (owner, 2026-11-04).** The IX8v checks use Setup.exe. The in-place updater case runs under IX8r. IX8q and IX8r are built before v1.0.
- **Release gate.** IX8v ships only after a released v0.2.2 that contains the session-aware updater check (branch `fix-update-service-block`): an unreadable Navo-named process blocks the restart only in the user's own Windows session. Without it `updater/apply.rs:196-202` blocks on any unreadable `navo*` image, `navo-index.exe` too, and the service runs in session 0. Proof before the IX8v release: the `v0.2.2` tag's `crates/navo-app/src/updater/apply.rs` has the session argument (`git show v0.2.2:crates/navo-app/src/updater/apply.rs`), and the v0.2.2 release on GitHub exists. Main at `71d87ce2` is v0.2.1 and has no such check. Users on v0.2.1 run Setup.exe by hand once (the owner was told).

#### IX8v1a — Remove cleans up

- **Read:** `installation/windows.rs:267-372`, `removal.rs`, `install_plan.rs:5-60,131-141,241-246`, `installation/verify.rs:86-113`; the Log line of 2026-11-02 on the leftover exe.
- **Change:** `installation/windows.rs` (`stop`, `remove_service`, `remove_binary`), `removal.rs`, `install_plan.rs`. `Product` gains `removable: &[&str]`, the images remove deletes: `navo-index.exe` and `navo.exe` for Navo, `ferrite.exe` for Ferrite. `Product::image` (the install image) stays `navo.exe` until IX8v3b switches it. `stop` follows the decided states. The file steps and the schedule run through a small trait, so tests use fakes for delete, rename and `MoveFileExW`.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index-service removal install_plan`: both names and their copies are deleted; an unknown file stays, is listed, and keeps its folder unscheduled; a refused delete renames and schedules the renamed path; a refused rename schedules the original path; a refused schedule fails; folders are scheduled after their files; each SCM state takes the decided path; a stop past 12 s fails with nothing deleted. Windows lint.
- **Stop if:** the "Access is denied" of 2026-11-02 is not a still-mapped image (for example an ACL from `Policy::Executable`); report with the ACL dump before changing permissions. Also stop if `OpenProcess(SYNCHRONIZE)` on the service process is refused for an elevated administrator in the VM.

#### IX8v1b — Deferred outcome through the CLI and the app

- **Read:** `elevation_result.rs`, `installer.rs`, `search_hooks/service.rs:76-92`; IX8v1a's outcome.
- **Change:** `elevation_result.rs`: code 6 and its reason; `from_exit_code(6)` returns a deferred result, not an error. `installer.rs`: `Removal::Deferred`, logged without a count because exit 6 cannot carry it; the remove deadline is a constant under `REMOVE_BUDGET`. `search_hooks/service.rs`: `report` maps a deferred result to `ServiceOutcome::Deferred` (added in IX8v1c's contract first, or as one line here if IX8v1c lands later).
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index-service elevation_result` (6 maps to deferred; 1 stays failed). `cargo nextest run -p navo-app installer search_hooks`: a deferred removal is not Skipped; a remove slower than the budget is Skipped with "no answer"; the deadline constant is below `REMOVE_BUDGET`.
- **Stop if:** the deferred result needs a change to `ElevationError` users outside these files.

#### IX8v1c — Deferred notice

- **Read:** `navo-ui/src/window_view/search_hooks.rs:15-50`, `window_view/settings_sheet/service.rs:40-70`, `navo-l10n/locales/en-US/settings.ftl:45-55`, `window_view/tests/settings_search.rs`.
- **Change:** `ServiceOutcome::Deferred`; `receive_index_service` shows `settings-search-service-deferred` = "The index service is removed. Windows deletes its last files at the next restart." as the notice.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-ui settings_search`: Deferred shows the notice and the status as Not installed.
- **Stop if:** the notice needs a new UI element.

#### IX8v2 — Pipe identity

- **Read:** `navo-index/src/pipe/version.rs`, `pipe/status.rs:36-52`, `pipe/windows.rs:56-75`, `pipe/unix.rs:15-25`, `pipe/version_tests.rs`, `pipe/tests/windows.rs`.
- **Change:** `Pipe::server_process_id()` on Windows (`GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` on the connected client handle); an error elsewhere. `Client::version_from_server(deadline) -> Result<(ServiceVersion, u32), PipeError>` asks over one connection and returns the server's id with the answer. `Client::version` keeps its contract.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index pipe::version`: the answer and the id come from the same connection; the old call still decodes `Older`. Windows test in `pipe/tests/windows.rs`: a client handle opened with `CreateFileW` on a test pipe returns the test server's process id. Passes in VM CI.
- **Stop if:** `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` fails on a client handle in the Windows test (Microsoft's parameter text names a `CreateNamedPipe` handle); report before any fallback. Also stop if the call needs a second connection or a wire change.

#### IX8v3a — Install steps

- **Read:** `install_plan.rs:192-352`, `install_plan_tests.rs`, `install_replace_tests.rs`, `install_rollback_tests.rs`, `upgrade.rs:25-94`.
- **Change:** `install_plan.rs`: for an existing service the steps are Copy, Stop, RenameOld, Replace, BackupSettings, Configure, Start, Verify, Answer; for a new one the same without Stop. `DeleteLegacy` runs after `DeleteOld`, past the commit point. `recover` runs `RemoveFresh` whenever the image was not there before, also for an existing service; `Restore` puts a backed-up `navo-index.exe` back. `upgrade.rs`: `AwaitNew` is the same Answer step.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index-service install_plan upgrade`: migration from `navo.exe` (no `navo-index.exe` yet) and reinstall over an existing `navo-index.exe` both run the decided order; Stop comes before RenameOld; a failure at each step from Stop to Answer restores the backup or removes the new image, restores the configuration and restarts a service that ran; `DeleteLegacy` never runs before Answer passes; a failed `DeleteLegacy` still succeeds with a warning.
- **Stop if:** a step needs a Windows call in `install_plan.rs`.

#### IX8v3b — Switch and migrate (Windows)

- **Read:** `installation/windows.rs:45-172`, `installation/verify.rs`, `install_check.rs`; IX8v1a's `stop`, IX8v2's `version_from_server`, IX8v3a's steps.
- **Change:** `installation/windows.rs`: copy source = the sibling `navo-index.exe` of the running `navo.exe`, refused when missing; `CopyPlan` for `navo-index.exe`; `binary_command` → `"…\Index\navo-index.exe" run`; `Configure` sets the description; the Answer and `DeleteLegacy` steps. `installation/verify.rs` and `install_check.rs`: Verify keeps the opened process handle and Answer checks through it, as decided; a miss is a `Mismatch` (exit 3). `navo-index/src/pipe/version.rs`: `SERVICE_REVISION` 2 → 3.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index-service installation install_check`: a server id that differs from the retained process, an exited retained process, an SCM id that changed, `Older` and revision 2 are each a mismatch; a missing sibling refuses before any step. Windows lint.
- **Stop if:** the work needs a production file beyond these four; split it, do not grow it.

#### IX8v4a — One change at a time

- **Read:** `mod.rs:72-111` (`Action`, `run`, `request_elevation`), `installation/windows.rs:45-64,267-271`; IX8v3b's Verify.
- **Change:** `change_lock.rs`: elevated changes exclusively open `%ProgramFiles%\Navo\change.lock` (`CreateFileW`, read/write, share mode 0, `OPEN_ALWAYS`), released on drop and process exit. Retry only sharing violations until the wait ends. Pin the protected executable parent before opening the file; a wrong ACL on an existing lock is rewritten, not refused, because the owner and folder are checked first. The app keeps `Local\\NavoIndexServiceChange`. An already-current install skips replacement but still cleans Ferrite.
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-index-service change_lock`: a second acquire waits and then gets the lock; a try acquire returns `None` while held; an abandoned mutex counts as acquired. An already-current install changes nothing (fake SCM).
- **Stop if:** the protected directory guard refuses the lock parent or the exclusive file open fails outside sharing contention.

#### IX8v4b — Upgrade path in the app

- **Read:** `version_step.rs`, `version_step_tests.rs`, `search_hooks/service.rs:40-150`, `mod.rs:113-141` (`ServiceQuery`, `query`), `scm/windows.rs:36-80`; IX8v4a's lock.
- **Change:** `version_step.rs`: the worker tries the local lock (held: no prompt, debug log), queries again under it, then prompts by the old rule; `is_updated` needs `Some(Known { revision >= own })`. `search_hooks/service.rs`: Install and Remove take the local lock (waiting) and recheck the status first; `status_from_query` returns `Outdated` also for `legacy_image`, in any state. `ServiceQuery` gains `legacy_image: bool` from the registered command (`scm/windows.rs`).
- **Check:** `cargo nextest run -p navo-app index_service::version_step search_hooks`: no answer after the prompt is not updated; `Known` 3 is; `Older` and 2 are not; a held lock gives no prompt; a recheck that finds the service current gives no prompt; a stopped or silent service on `navo.exe` is `Outdated`; a stopped service on `navo-index.exe` stays `Stopped`.
- **Stop if:** reading the registered command needs elevation in the VM.

#### IX8v VM checks

Run on the Windows VM after IX8v4b, before the release. Start state: v0.2.2 installed by Setup.exe, the service registered as `navo.exe --index-service run` and running. N+1 is the IX8v build, installed by its Setup.exe over v0.2.2 (D22f: the same path as an in-place update).

1. First start of N+1: exactly one UAC prompt. Then Task Manager → Details shows `navo-index.exe` with the description "Navo Index"; Services shows the description line; `Index` holds only `navo-index.exe`; Settings → Search shows Running; the service answers revision 3. A second start shows no prompt.
2. Declined prompt: no second prompt at the next start; Settings → Search shows "Older than Navo" with Update; Update gives one prompt and the state of check 1. 2b: two profiles started at the same time over the old service give one prompt in total.
3. Old service stopped before the first start: no prompt; Settings shows Update; Update migrates and the service runs.
4. Failure and rollback. 4a: the sibling `navo-index.exe` replaced by a copy of the v0.2.2 `navo.exe` (it refuses `run`): the install exits nonzero, the registered command is `navo.exe` again with its description and recovery, the old service runs, `navo-index.exe` is gone from `Index`. 4b: the sibling replaced by an IX8u build of `navo-index.exe` (revision 2): exit 3, the same restored state. 4c: reinstall over a migrated service with 4a's broken sibling: the previous `navo-index.exe` is back and runs.
5. Remove from Settings, then uninstall: no "Navo Index" service, no `Index` folder, no `navo.exe` or `navo-index.exe` left; the uninstall log shows Removed within 25 s.
6. Remove with `Index\navo-index.exe` held open but allowing rename: Settings shows the deferred notice; the renamed file and then the Index folder are gone after a reboot. When both delete and rename are refused, exit 7, 8 or 9 (`LockedImage`); the image path is never scheduled.
7. Hold the old `Global\\NavoIndexServiceChange` name from a non-elevated process: elevated install still completes. Contend on `change.lock`: install waits at most 60 s; remove shares its 20 s deadline with the remaining cleanup.

The in-place updater case (v0.2.2 → N+1 through the updater, with the service running) runs under IX8r before v1.0. Owner: check 1 on a Windows PC (Task Manager).

**Order.** Updated 2026-11-04. IX8a–IX8f and IX8h–IX8p have shipped. IX8g is dropped and is no dependency. IX8u is being built. IX8v2 touches only `navo-index` and can start at once. IX8u → IX8v1a → IX8v1b → IX8v1c; IX8v1a → IX8v3a; IX8v2 + IX8v3a → IX8v3b → IX8v4a → IX8v4b. The IX8v VM checks run after IX8v4b and IX8v1c. IX8o → IX8q → IX8r → IX8s → IX8t; IX8q and IX8r are built before v1.0. No release between IX8v1a and IX8v4b: the first release with revision 3 carries all of them. It ships only after a released v0.2.2 that contains the session-aware updater check (release gate in the IX8v section). One cargo gate at a time (D11).

**Done when.**

- An installed build N finds N+1, downloads it in the background and starts as N+1 at the next start (IX8r case 1; then a real release on the owner's PC).
- The index service follows with one UAC prompt only when its revision changed (IX8r case 8; owner).
- Task Manager shows the service as "Navo Index" (`navo-index.exe`), not a second `navo.exe`; an install over the old `navo.exe --index-service run` registration migrates with that one prompt and leaves no `navo.exe` in `Index`; remove leaves no exe or folder, or reports a reboot delete that leaves none after the reboot (IX8v VM checks; IX8r case 9; owner).
- A service update counts only when the service answers the new revision from its own process (IX8v3b, IX8v4b).
- The portable zip and the bare exe send no update request (IX8r case 6; IX8b tests).
- Every IX8r case passes; rate limits pass in unit tests.
- One review verdict, the full gate once, VM CI green, docs updated.

#### S0 — Recently deleted, three directions

- **Change:** three genuinely different directions for the sheet: list rows (name, original folder, size, deleted when, expires in), grouping (by day, by volume or flat), Restore and Delete now, empty state, a drive that cannot retain, a volume over its cap. Calm by default (D58). Keys shown next to actions.
- **Check:** the prototype opens with a picker; both themes; 390 px and desktop screenshots looked at.
- **Stop if:** a direction needs data the catalog does not hold (`RetainedRow` fields above).

#### S1 — Retention on by default

- **Change:** `Start.experimental_retention` becomes `Start.retention`, default `true`. `--no-retention` sets it `false`. `--experimental-retention` is accepted and ignored for one release, with a log line. The one-instance request keeps its wire field name; a mismatch is still refused.
- **Check:** tests: no flag → `retention == true`; `--no-retention` → `false`; both flags → `false`; a mismatched second instance is refused.
- **Stop if:** any code path outside `cli.rs`, `main.rs` and the instance request reads the old field name.

- **Decided 2026-10-28 (S1 senior review: HOLD).** The S1 diff is correct (kept on branch `held/s1-retention-default`), but the default flip turns on coordinated mode with no benefit until S2, and has costs. S1 merges together with S2, after these atoms:
  - **S1a** (Opus, done 2026-10-28): with retention off (`--no-retention` safe mode), `guard`, `guard_paths`, `claim_write` and the top-level `check_mutation` still refuse reserved paths (retained containers, profile folder), without leases or the catalog (D2).
  - **S1b** (Opus, done 2026-10-28): the exclusive user-wide mutation lock gets a deadline and a visible "waiting for another operation" on the job card; the lock is scoped per profile, not per user; editor save / new file / F2 rename do not fail with AccessDenied during a long delete.
  - **S1c** (Sol, done 2026-10-28 in reduced form: per-plan parent cache, folders listed once, ignored timing tests; the skip-scan shortcut was dropped as unprovable — unregistered retained folders, held files in the journal, safe mode; S1f (Opus) then cut the 100k-copy pre-check from 2.4 s to 0.23 s in release): pre-checks canonicalise each parent once per plan; plain renames skip the descendant scan when no retained container can be inside; a Windows performance sample (100k files copy, same-drive folder move) before the flip.
  - **S1d** (Opus, done 2026-10-28): a TOML error in navo.toml blocks only Delete and overwriting Copy/Move (or shows a start-up settings notice), not every copy.
  - **S1e** (Sol, done with S1): the retention-mode mismatch refusal shows a message box on Windows; blocked-mutation notices name `--no-retention`.
  - Test gaps to close with S1: `default_retention_reaches_launch` is unix-only; no test for a default-mode request to a running `--no-retention` instance; `check_store` receives `retention`. `--experimental-retention` twice should not be an error.

#### S2 — Defaults

- **Change:** `RetentionData::default()` per Q1 (architect default: `retain_deletes = true`, `retain_overwrites = true`, `days = 3`, `max_volume_mib = 1024`, `min_free_mib = 256`). Update the snapshot.
- **Check:** settings tests pass; the snapshot diff shows only the two flags.

#### S3 — Drives that cannot retain

- **Change:** before building a retained delete plan, ask the target provider for `rename_atomic` and file identity support. Without them, build the ordinary permanent-delete plan and add the D3 line (`delete-cannot-retain`) to its confirmation. Overwrites on such drives keep today's behaviour and say nothing.
- **Check:** tests with `FakeFs` without atomic rename: confirmation shows the line; Cancel creates no job; a supporting provider still retains.
- **Stop if:** the provider capability is not reachable from the UI layer without a new hook; report the shape.

#### S4 — Contract for retained entries

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/browser/retained.rs`: `RetainedRow { id: RetentionId, original: Location, kind, bytes: u64, deleted_at_ms: i64, expires_at_ms: i64, state: RetainedState }`, `RetainedState { Ready, Busy, NeedsAttention }`, trait `RetainedHooks: Send + Sync { fn list(&self, after: Option<RetentionId>, limit: u16) -> Result<Vec<RetainedRow>, String>; fn restore(&self, id) -> Result<Location, String>; fn purge(&self, id) -> Result<(), String>; }`. A `NoRetained` default that returns empty lists.
- **Check:** crate check; `NoRetained` lists nothing.

#### S5 — Hooks in the app

- **Change:** `navo-app/src/retained_hooks.rs` implements S4 on `Vault` and `RetentionCatalog`. Every call runs off the UI thread and takes the exclusive mutation lease. `restore` targets the original path; on a collision it uses the " (restored)" name (D4). Errors are Fluent strings. Wire it into `gui.rs` next to the rename hooks.
- **Check:** tests on a temp profile: list pages in id order; restore to a free path; restore with a collision keeps both; purge of a pinned entry refuses with a message; a running job's entry shows `Busy`.
- **Stop if:** `Vault` cannot be shared with the job engine's instance (one `retention.lock` per profile); report before adding a second owner.

#### S6 — Ctrl+Z restores

- **Change:** `UndoOffer` gets `kind`. A finished retained delete or overwrite job sets `Retained(ids)`. `undo_last` calls `RetainedHooks::restore` per id and reports partial results in the status line.
- **Check:** tests: Shift+Del then Ctrl+Z restores both files; a rename undo still works; an entry purged in between is reported, the rest restored.

#### S7 — Maintenance timer

- **Change:** run `maintain_volume` for each volume with live entries once after start-up recovery, then every 6 hours, on a background thread with the lease and the existing bounds. Skip while a job holds the lease; try again in 10 minutes.
- **Check:** tests with an injected clock: start-up pass runs; a 6 h tick runs; a held lease defers.

#### S8 — Prune terminal rows

- **Change:** delete `Restored`, `Purged` and `Aborted` rows whose container cleanup is acknowledged and whose terminal time is older than 30 days, at most 1,000 per call, in one short transaction. Called from the S7 pass.
- **Check:** tests: old acknowledged rows go; unacknowledged and live rows stay; a job referenced by a live row is never pruned.

#### S9 — The sheet

- **Change:** the picked R1 direction (D86: by day) on `side_sheet`. Icons sit centred on the first text line of each row and each day header; the screenshot check at 390 px and desktop, both themes, includes a zoomed crop that shows icon and text aligned. Up and Down move, Enter restores, Delete asks and purges, Esc closes. Pages of 200 rows load on scroll through `RetainedHooks::list`. Calls run off the UI thread.
- **Check:** headless tests: rows render from a fake hook; Enter calls restore; Delete opens the confirmation; empty state text.
- **Stop if:** the side sheet cannot host a virtual list; report.

#### S10 — Command and wiring

- **Change:** `files.recently-deleted` in the registry, the File menu and Ctrl+K; no default key. Opens the S9 sheet with the app's hooks. Update the command snapshots.
- **Check:** snapshot diffs show one new command; a test opens the sheet from the command.

#### S11 — Settings section

- **Change:** `Section::Safety` ("Safety net", icon `Undo2` or the nearest existing one) with `RetainDeletes`, `RetainOverwrites` (flags), `RetentionDays` (choices 1–7), `RetentionCapMib`, `RetentionMinFreeMib` (numbers). Keys `retention.*`. One help sentence each; the days field says "Not a backup".
- **Check:** settings tests: the section lists five fields; a change writes the right key; an invalid cap shows the store's error.
- **Stop if:** a fourth code file is needed; report which.

#### S12 — Windows runtime checks

- **Change:** the Windows CI test job runs `navo-retention` and the `navo-app` retention and deletion tests natively (they are skipped there today if so).
- **Check:** the CI log on `windows-latest` lists them as passed.

#### V2 — Follow, reproduced

- **Change:** a Windows-only test starts a child writer process on a temp file opened by `PagedFile`. Case 1 appends in place. Case 2 writes a temp file and replaces the original, as VS Code saves. Each case checks that `len()` grows and `take_changed()` turns true within 3 s. The report names the root cause with evidence (for example a share mode, a stale handle or `same_identity` on NTFS).
- **Check:** at least one case fails on `windows-latest` for the named cause; Linux skips the test.
- **Stop if:** both cases pass on CI; then report and ask the owner for the exact writer.

#### V3 — Follow, fixed

- **Change:** the smallest fix for the cause V2 proved, inside `navo-fs/src/paged/`. Nothing in the viewer changes unless V2 names it.
- **Check:** V2's test and the existing follow tests pass.

#### V4–V6 — Done

V4 and V5 built `DescRow` rows for DBC and A2L; V6 registered `DbcKind` and `A2lKind` with the flat table body. V7a–V7k replace the table; V7k removes the rows.

#### V7a — DBC outline

- **Change:** new `navo-automotive/src/description/outline.rs`, exported from `description/mod.rs` and the crate root:
  - `enum ItemRef { Node(u32), Message(u32), Signal { message: u32, signal: u16 }, Characteristic(u32), Measurement(u32), Compu(u32) }` (indexes into the model's vectors).
  - `struct OutlineRow { depth: u8, group: bool, item: Option<ItemRef>, label: Arc<str>, aux: Arc<str>, line: u64, search: Arc<str> }`; `search` is the lowercased label, aux and, for messages, the hex ID.
  - `struct Outline { rows: Vec<OutlineRow> }` with `item_at_line(&self, line: u64) -> Option<usize>` (the item row with the greatest `line` ≤ `line`) and `parent(&self, row: usize) -> Option<usize>`.
  - `Signal` gains `line: u64` (the `SG_` line; `dbc/convert.rs` has it in the `(line, is_signal)` context).
  - New `navo-automotive/src/dbc/outline.rs`: `dbc::outline(&DbcModel) -> Outline`. Depth 0: one group per sender node in `model.nodes` order (label: node name; aux: "N msg"); messages whose sender is empty, `Vector__XXX` or not in `nodes` go under a last group "No sender". Depth 1: messages in file order (label: `0x1A0 VCU_TorqueReq`, with " (extended)" for extended IDs; aux: "20 ms" or empty). Depth 2: signals in file order (label: name; aux: `start|length unit`, for example `16|3`).
- **Check:** `tests/dbc_outline.rs` on a small DBC with two nodes, a message without sender and a multiplexed signal: row order and depths; aux texts; `item_at_line` of a signal's line returns that signal's row; `parent` of a signal is its message.

#### V7b — A2L outline and links

- **Change:** new `navo-automotive/src/a2l/outline.rs`: `a2l::outline(&A2lModel) -> Outline`. Row 0 is an info row (no item, not a group): "Module <name> · XCP" (or CCP, or nothing) from `modules[0]`. Then group "Characteristics (N)" with sub-groups by kind in the order MAP, CURVE, VAL_BLK, VALUE, then other kinds alphabetically (label: kind; aux: count); items: label name, aux address in hex. Group "Measurements (N)": label name, aux "<data type> <unit>". Group "Compu methods (N)": label name, aux "<type> · <users>". Empty groups are left out. `a2l::links(&A2lModel) -> Links { users: Vec<Vec<ItemRef>>, missing: Vec<ItemRef> }`: `users[i]` are the characteristics and measurements whose `conversion` names compu method i; `missing` are items whose conversion is neither `NO_COMPU_METHOD` nor a known name.
- **Check:** `tests/a2l_outline.rs`: group order, kind sub-groups, counts, a measurement with a missing compu method in `missing`, `users` of a shared method lists both items.

#### V7c — DBC bit layout and formula

- **Change:** new `navo-automotive/src/dbc/bits.rs`, exported from `dbc/mod.rs`:
  - `struct BitLayout { bytes: u8, cells: Vec<BitCell>, problems: Vec<String> }`, `struct BitCell { signals: Vec<u16> }` (empty for a free bit), `fn cell(&self, byte: u8, bit: u8) -> &BitCell` with bit 0 the least significant.
  - `fn bit_layout(message: &Message) -> BitLayout`: `bytes` is the DLC (CAN FD up to 64). Intel signals take bits `start..start+length` in linear order. Motorola signals start at their most significant bit and walk the DBC sawtooth: next bit is `bit - 1`, except when `bit % 8 == 0`, then `bit + 15`. A bit past the DLC adds the problem "<signal> runs past byte <n>". Two signals on one bit add "<a> and <b> overlap at bit <n>", unless both are multiplexed with different values (`Multiplexing::Value(a)` vs `Value(b)`).
  - `fn formula(signal: &Signal) -> String`: "phys = raw × <factor> + <offset>" plus " (signed 16 bit)", " (unsigned 3 bit)" or " (float 32 bit)"; factors and offsets print like `rows.rs` numbers (shortest form).
- **Check:** `tests/dbc_bits.rs`: an Intel 16-bit signal at 0 fills bytes 0 and 1; a Motorola 12-bit signal at start 7 fills bit 7…0 of byte 0 and 7…4 of byte 1; an overlap is reported; two multiplexed signals with different values are not; a signal past DLC 2 is reported; formulas for signed, unsigned and float.

#### V7d — Description view

- **Change:** new `description/mod.rs` and `description/outline.rs` under the viewer; `kinds/description.rs` changes `load` and `attach`.
  - `load` builds `DescriptionFile { len, outline: Arc<Outline>, model: Arc<DescModel> }`, `enum DescModel { Dbc(DbcModel), A2l(A2lModel, Links) }`, off the UI thread as today. `flatten` goes away.
  - `attach` creates `DescriptionView`: the outline as a virtual list in a left side (as `MapView`'s navigator) and an inspector area on the right that shows "Pick a message or a signal." (DBC) or "Pick a characteristic or a measurement." (A2L) until V7f and V7g fill it.
  - Rows draw with 18 px indent per depth, a chevron on groups, label in mono, aux right-aligned and muted, as in the prototype's `.dB .tn` rows.
  - Keys: Up/Down and J/K move; Home/End; Left folds a group or goes to the parent; Right unfolds; Enter on a group toggles it; Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I toggle the outline and the inspector (D58), with the Simple/Rich/Custom modes of the View menu.
  - The selected item and the fold state survive a re-layout and a window resize.
- **Check:** headless tests: a DBC opens with three groups unfolded; Left on a signal selects its message; Left on a message folds it; Ctrl+B hides the outline; an A2L opens with its info row and groups.
- **Stop if:** a fourth code file is needed; report which.

#### V7e — Find in the outline

- **Change:** new `description/find.rs` and `description/keys.rs`. A find box tops the outline; `/` or Ctrl+F focuses it. A row is a hit when its `search` contains the lowercased text. The box shows "N hits". Enter or N selects the next hit, Shift+Enter or Shift+N the previous; selecting unfolds its parents. Chip "Only matches" hides rows that are not hits and not ancestors of hits. Chip "Collapse all" folds every group. Esc in the box clears it and returns focus to the outline. The outline's scrollbar shows ticks for hits and the selected row through `search/overview.rs` `Ticks`.

For a message: tiles CAN ID, DLC, Cycle, Sender, Signals; the bit grid; the signal list; the comment. For a node: the messages it sends. The two inner columns stack below 720 px of viewer width.

## B4 · Workspaces and quick actions

Workspaces follow D14 and D17 (owner pick D82, direction D of `.crew/design/workspaces/index.html`: the tray). Holding Alt already arms the modifier-hints drawer through `Hold` (`navo-ui/src/modifier_hints/hold.rs:1-109`), fed by `on_modifiers_changed` (`navo-ui/src/window_view/mod.rs:394-398`); the tray reuses that timing. Alt+1, Alt+3 and Alt+5 are viewer view keys (`commands/keymap.toml:994-1002`); Alt+Left and Alt+Right are Back and Forward (`keymap.toml:317-322`); Ctrl+S is bound only in multi-rename and the viewer (`keymap.toml:927,981`); Alt+N and Alt+Shift+Left/Right are free.

Order: W1 starts now. W1 → W2, W3. W2 + Q12 + Q13 → W4 → W5 → W6, W7. W1 and tiling TL4 (`plans/2026-10-27-tiling.md`) both change `navo-core/src/state/window.rs`: W1 runs first; TL4 reads W1's shape.

#### W1 — Workspaces in the core

- **Change:** new `navo-core/src/state/workspace.rs`. `Snapshot` holds every field of `Window` except `id`, `placement` and the workspace list itself (today: layout, panes, last marks; after tiling TL4 also `target`). `Workspace { name: String, saved: Snapshot, live: Option<Snapshot> }`; `live` is `None` for the active workspace (its live state is the window itself) and for a workspace with no draft. `Window` gains `workspaces: Vec<Workspace>` (1 to 9) and `active: usize`. A new window has one workspace "Workspace 1" saved from its first state.
  - `switch(n)`: n is 1-based; beyond the list → `StateError::NoWorkspace(n)` ("There is no workspace n."). Leaving stores the window's state as the left workspace's `live` when it differs from `saved` (or as `saved` when `SAVE_ON_SWITCH` is true; the constant is `false` per Q12). Entering restores `live` or else `saved`. Switching to the active workspace does nothing.
  - `new(name)`: refuses at nine; copies the current state as `saved` of a new workspace appended to the list and switches to it. The default name is "Workspace <n>".
  - `rename(n, name)` (1–40 characters, trimmed), `move(n, by)` (swaps with the neighbour; the active index follows), `delete(n)` (refuses the last one; keeps the deleted workspace and its index for one `restore()`), `save()` (saved = current state), `discard(n)` (drops the draft; for the active one, restores `saved` into the window).
  - `changes(&Snapshot, &Snapshot) -> u16`: one per pane whose tab folders or active tab differ by position, plus one when the layout tree or a ratio differs.
  - Pane and tab ids stay unique across all workspaces: restoring a snapshot keeps its ids; `discard` and `restore` never duplicate ids that are live elsewhere.
  - Effects list the visible tabs again after a switch.
- **Check:** tests: switch 1 → 2 → 1 restores tabs, focus and layout; a change in workspace 1 survives a switch as a draft with `changes == 1`; `save` clears it; `discard` restores the saved tabs; `new` at nine errors; `delete` then `restore` puts it back at its index; `switch(5)` with three workspaces errors; a property test keeps pane and tab ids unique after random operations.
- **Stop if:** `PaneId` or `TabId` uniqueness needs a change outside `state/`; report the shape.

#### W2 — Workspace commands

- **Change:** commands and ids `workspace.switch n=<1–9>`, `workspace.new [name=<text>]`, `workspace.rename n=<1–9> name=<text>`, `workspace.move n=<1–9> by=<-1|1>`, `workspace.delete n=<1–9>`, `workspace.restore`, `workspace.save`, `workspace.discard n=<1–9>`, applied through W1 in a new `apply/workspace.rs`. Five files with one short arm each, as C1; that is allowed here.
- **Check:** parse and print round-trip each; `n=0` and `n=10` are parse errors; apply tests for switch and new.

#### W3 — Workspaces saved

- **Change:** `SESSION_VERSION` becomes 3. `WindowState` gains `workspaces: Vec<WorkspaceState { name, saved: SnapshotState, live: Option<SnapshotState> }>` and `active_workspace: u8`, with serde defaults. A V2 file decodes as one workspace "Workspace 1" saved from the window's layout and panes. A V4 file is refused as today. Limits: at most 9 workspaces, names at most 40 characters; a broken workspace entry is dropped with a log line, the rest load.
- **Check:** round-trip test with two workspaces and a draft; a V2 fixture loads as one workspace; a file with 12 workspaces keeps the first 9.

#### W4 — Workspace keys

- **Change:** new `window_view/workspace_keys.rs`, wired from `track_modifiers` and the file-list key context.
  - Holding Alt alone in the file list for the modifier-hints delay shows the tray (W5); it never shows in a viewer, a dialog or Ctrl+K.
  - Alt+1…9 shows the tray at once and marks workspace n as previewed (beyond the list: releasing Alt makes a new workspace from the current layout, as Alt+N does, and switches to it; the tray previews it as "New workspace", per Q13). Releasing Alt switches to the previewed workspace. Esc while Alt is held cancels the preview and stays. Alt+n on the active workspace previews nothing.
  - Alt+N runs `workspace.new`. Ctrl+S runs `workspace.save`. Alt+Shift+Left/Right run `workspace.move` on the active workspace. Alt+Left/Right keep Back and Forward.
  - Losing window focus while Alt is held cancels the preview.
  - Command snapshots gain the new commands and keys.
- **Check:** window tests with simulated modifier events: Alt+2 then Alt release switches; Alt+2, Esc, release stays; holding Alt alone past the delay shows the tray; Alt+Left still goes back; snapshots reviewed.
- **Stop if:** GPUI does not report the Alt release while another key is held; report the event sequence.

- **Change:** new `window_view/workspace_tray.rs`: a layer above the hints drawer and the status bar, full window width, per the prototype's `trayD`. Header "Workspaces" with the hint "Hold Alt, press a number to preview, release Alt to switch. Esc stays." and a close button. One card per workspace: a mini layout drawn from `Layout::rects()` of its saved or live snapshot, each tile with its active folder's last segment; the number key cap, the name, "open" on the active one; the previewed card outlined with the accent. A last card "New from current layout · Alt+N". While a preview is set, a ribbon in the title bar says "Previewing <name> · release Alt to switch · Esc to stay". Clicking a card switches and closes the tray. At 390 px the cards scroll sideways. Fades in 80 ms (fade only); reduced motion shows it at once.
- **Check:** tests: three workspaces render three cards plus "New"; the active one says "open"; Alt+2 outlines card 2 and shows the ribbon; a click on card 3 switches.

Delete runs at once and shows "Deleted <name> · Ctrl+Z restores it" in the status line; Ctrl+Z in the file list runs `workspace.restore` when the last undoable action was that delete (`window_view/undo.rs` offers it next to rename undo).
