## OR1–OR2 · Git at a glance

The owner asked for OR1 and OR2 on 2026-10-30. D85 had put Git in v1.2 as a module; where this section ships is QM1. It ships as two pre-releases: **OR1** badges (GS1–GS9, GH1, GH2, GU0–GU2, GU5, GR1, GD1), then **OR2** the menu and the user's Git tool (GT0–GT4, GH3, GU3, GU4, GU6, GX1, GR2, GD2). Appetite: about two weeks of worker runs. If it runs over, the gix fallback (GS5) and the background fetch (GS8, GS9) are cut first. Every D100 entry below is **proposed, Manwë to confirm**; no atom starts before that, except GT0 and GU0, which decide nothing.

What exists (checked at `d2c40672`, paths under `crates/`): `navo-git` has the `GitSource` trait (read-only refs, trees, blobs, changes; `lib.rs:312-330`), `CliGit` and `GixGit`, and `choose` (`choose.rs:79-114`). `Choice::for_work(Work::WorktreeStatus)` already sends worktree status to the CLI whenever Git ≥ 2.36 is found, under every engine setting. `GixGit::changes` is unsupported (`gix_source.rs:166`), and `gix` is built without its `status` feature (`navo-git/Cargo.toml`). The CLI already spawns `<git --exec-path>/git.exe` (`cli/process.rs:49-73`) with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` (`process.rs:117-136`). It has a total timeout but no cancel and no output cap (`process.rs:138-162,238-260`). No `git-engine` setting exists in `navo-store` yet; P42 U2 (Aulë) plans `[compare] git-engine`. `navo-fs/src/git/` uses `CliGit` for `repo@rev` listings. Nothing shows Git status. The file list draws a check column left of the icon (`navo-ui/src/file_list/row.rs:36-74,240-291`). `file_list/mod.rs` has 389 lines, `window_view/pane.rs` 394, `settings/values.rs` 396, `browser/mod.rs` 406 (already over 400). The terminal launcher is the pattern for external programs (`navo-app/src/terminal/mod.rs:12-75,185-244`). It also holds a private HKCU registry reader (`terminal/system.rs:83-127`). `drive_class(&Location)` tells network drives (`navo-fs/src/drive_class.rs:16-25`). Icons come from the gpui-kit Lucide catalogue through `icon_assets!(NavoIcons, …)` (`navo-ui/src/assets.rs`). Its `git-branch`, `git-merge-conflict`, `circle-check`, `arrow-up`, `arrow-down` and `arrow-up-down` icons are available. Alt+G is bound only in the map viewer (`space.next_grouping`). On `part-compare-engine`, J1b adds `navo-git/src/cli/filtered_stream.rs` (streamed read with kill on cancel), so GS2 waits for it to reach `main`.

### What the user gets (spec)

- A local folder listing that holds Git repositories shows a badge on each repository folder. The badge sits in a gutter left of the folder icon. A listing without a repository has no gutter, so nothing in it moves.
- The badge shows one state: clean, modified, ahead, behind, ahead and behind, conflict, or unknown (with the reason). The last known state shows at once, faint while it is checked again, then the fresh one fades in (about 80 ms).
- The tooltip and the spoken name give the branch and its upstream, "3 ahead · 2 behind · fetched 2 h ago" and "4 changed, 1 new, 0 conflicts". An unknown badge says why: timed out, Git not installed, Git does not trust this folder, network drive skipped.
- A click on the badge, or Alt+G on a repository row, opens a menu. Its head shows the branch, the upstream, ahead and behind, and the last fetch. Below it are Commit…, Pull, Push, Log and Diff, each naming the tool ("Commit in TortoiseGit"). Items the tool cannot do do not show.
- With no tool set, Diff opens Navo's compare view: the working tree against HEAD.
- Settings → Git holds these rows:
  - Show Git status in the file list (on).
  - Git tool (Auto, which shows what it found; each detected tool with its path; Git command line; Other with a command; None).
  - Fetch in the background (off) and Every N minutes (15).
  - Network drives (off).
- Ctrl+K gains Git: Commit, Pull, Push, Log, Diff and Git menu for the repository under the cursor.

### Decisions for OR1–OR2

D100 holds the owner requests OR1 and OR2 applied to this plan. Each entry is **proposed, Manwë to confirm**. After confirmation, a worker that finds one wrong stops and reports.

- D100a — **Boundary.** New modules in `navo-git`: `status/` (model, parser, operation), `trust.rs` (guard), `cli/status.rs`, `cli/remote.rs`, `gix_source/status.rs`. `GitSource` does not change; status is its own trait `GitStatus` (QA3). The scanner, the fetch timer, tool detection, launching and wiring live in a new `navo-app/src/git/`. Registry reads move to `navo-shell` (GT1). `navo-ui` sees only `GitHooks` with its own row types, as with `RetainedHooks`. With `[git] badges = false`, Navo probes nothing and starts no thread and no process, and the Git commands are hidden. The feature joins the module registry when B12 lands.
- D100b — **Which folders get a badge.** Only folder rows of a local listing get one, and only when `<row>/.git` exists (a folder, or a file for worktrees and submodules). Archives, `repo@rev` listings, results tabs and Recently deleted get none. The probe looks one level deep, at the listed folder's children. The listed folder itself and folders inside a repository get no badge in the beta (Q29). Network drives (`DriveClass::Network`) are skipped unless `[git] network` is on; removable drives are included.
- D100c — **States.**
  - Model: `RepoStatus { head: Head::{Branch(String), Detached(ObjectId), Unborn(String)}, upstream: Option<Upstream { name, ahead: u32, behind: u32 }>, changed: u32, untracked: u32, conflicted: u32, operation: Option<Operation::{Merge, Rebase, CherryPick, Revert, Bisect}>, capped: bool, fetched_ms: Option<i64> }`.
  - One badge, highest first:
    1. `Conflict`: `conflicted > 0`, or a merge, rebase, cherry-pick or revert is in progress.
    2. `Modified`: `changed + untracked > 0` (Q27).
    3. `Diverged`: ahead and behind both above 0.
    4. `Ahead`.
    5. `Behind`.
    6. `Clean`.
    7. `Unknown(Reason::{TimedOut, NoGit, NotTrusted, Failed})`.
  - Ahead and behind compare HEAD with the local remote-tracking ref; nothing goes over the network. That is "behind since the last fetch". `fetched_ms` is the modified time of `FETCH_HEAD`. The tooltip always gives ahead and behind, even when the badge shows Modified.
- D100d — **How status is read.**
  - With Git ≥ 2.36 found (`Choice::for_work(Work::WorktreeStatus)`), Navo runs `git -c core.fsmonitor=false <D100e guards> status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z --untracked-files=normal --ignore-submodules=dirty --no-renames`, through the existing `base_command`.
  - stdout is read as a stream up to 8 MiB. At the cap Navo kills git, keeps the counts so far and sets `capped = true`. Conflicts then come from `git ls-files -u -z`, which stops at the first record.
  - The operation comes from marker files in the Git directory: `MERGE_HEAD`, `rebase-merge/`, `rebase-apply/`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD` and `BISECT_LOG`.
  - Without Git, `gix` status is used only if the GS5 trial passes. Otherwise the badge is `Unknown(NoGit)` ("Install Git to see changes"), and ahead and behind still come from gix refs.
  - Status never writes: `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` keeps `.git/index` untouched.
- D100e — **A repository's own config never runs a program in the background.**
  - Navo reads the repository-local config with `gix-config`: `.git/config`, `config.worktree` and their includes. Global and system config belong to the user and stay in force.
  - Every `filter.<name>` found there is neutralised on every background call: `-c filter.<name>.clean= -c filter.<name>.smudge= -c filter.<name>.process= -c filter.<name>.required=false`.
  - If the local config sets any key on the guard list, the repository is *guarded*, and the background fetch skips it. The guard list: `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `core.sshCommand`, `core.gitProxy`, `core.askPass`, `credential.helper`, `credential.<url>.helper`, `remote.<name>.uploadpack`, `remote.<name>.receivepack`, `diff.external`, `diff.<driver>.textconv`, `diff.<driver>.command`, `gpg.program`, `gpg.<format>.program`, `uploadpack.packObjectsHook`, `filter.*`.
  - Pull and Push that the user starts (D100g) run with the normal config. Their confirmation names the guarded keys.
  - When Git refuses an unsafe directory, the badge is `Unknown(NotTrusted)`. Navo never adds a `safe.directory` entry.
- D100f — **Limits and memory.**
  - Per listing: the probe checks at most 2,000 folder rows and stops after 500 ms. At most 64 repositories are scanned, rows on screen first.
  - App-wide: at most 2 Git processes run at once.
  - Timeouts: status 10 s, `ls-files -u` 5 s, fetch 60 s, Pull and Push 120 s.
  - The request queue holds 256; when it is full, the oldest listing's requests go first.
  - The last status of 256 repositories is kept in memory (LRU), never on disk.
  - Memory: steady at most 2 MiB (cache and queue); at most 8 MiB more per running status (the output cap), so 16 MiB at peak. One scanner thread, plus the two reader threads `process.rs` starts for each child.
  - A new scan starts when:
    - a listing opens or refreshes (F5, a watch event);
    - the window gets focus again and the status is over 60 s old;
    - a Navo fetch, pull or push ends;
    - a launched tool process exits.
  - Leaving a folder cancels its queued scans and kills its running Git children within 100 ms.
  - A timed-out repository is not tried again until one of the events above.
- D100g — **What Navo runs, and what it never runs.**

  | When | Navo runs | Writes into the repository |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Always, in the background | `git --version`, `git --exec-path` (once per session); `git status …` and `git ls-files -u -z` (D100d); gix reads of refs, `HEAD` and config | never |
  | Only with `[git] fetch` on (off by default, Q28) | `git fetch --quiet --no-recurse-submodules <upstream remote>` with `-c credential.interactive=never`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=never`; never for a guarded repository | remote-tracking refs, `FETCH_HEAD`, objects |
  | On a click, tool = Git command line, after a confirmation (Q30) | Pull: `git -c core.hooksPath= fetch --no-tags --no-prune --no-recurse-submodules --write-fetch-head --refmap= <remote> refs/heads/<upstream branch>`, which writes no ref, only `FETCH_HEAD`; then the confirmation is checked again (branch, HEAD, upstream, remote, config values); then `git merge --ff-only <commit from FETCH_HEAD>`; then `git -c core.hooksPath= update-ref <tracking ref> <that commit> <its value before the fetch>`. Push: `git push <remote> HEAD:refs/heads/<upstream branch>`, the current branch only. A run names the single-use id of the confirmation it shows; an older or reused id runs nothing | yes, what the user confirmed |
  | On a click, any other tool | starts the tool with the repository path and the action | the tool does, under the user's eye |

  Navo never runs commit, add, stash, reset, checkout, switch, merge (other than the fast-forward of a confirmed Pull), rebase, clean, branch or tag changes, config writes or gc. It never runs `push` with `--force`, `--force-with-lease`, `--set-upstream`, `--tags`, `--delete` or `--mirror`. It never edits `safe.directory`. A Pull that is not a fast-forward stops with "Pull needs a merge; use your Git tool" and leaves the worktree untouched. This amends D70's "Navo never writes into `.git`" for the background fetch and user-confirmed Pull and Push only (Q28, Q30).
- D100h — **The user's Git tool.**
  - Settings: `[git] tool` = `auto` (default), `none`, `tortoisegit`, `fork`, `github-desktop`, `sourcetree`, `gitkraken`, `git-cli` or `custom`. `custom` uses `tool-command`, a template with `{path}` and `{action}`.
  - `auto` picks the first GUI tool found in that order. It never picks `git-cli` (D100g).
  - Detection on Windows:
    - TortoiseGit: `HKLM\SOFTWARE\TortoiseGit` value `ProcPath` (64-bit view), then `%ProgramFiles%\TortoiseGit\bin\TortoiseGitProc.exe`.
    - Fork, GitHub Desktop, SourceTree and GitKraken: their paths under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, from GT0.
    - Git: `find_git` plus `--exec-path`.
    - Detection runs once per session on a worker, and again when Settings → Git opens.
    - On Linux: `git-cli` and `custom` only.
  - Actions:
    - TortoiseGit: `/command:commit|pull|push|log /path:"<repo>"`; Diff is `/command:repostatus` ("Check for modifications").
    - Tools that can only open a repository (per GT0) offer "Open in <tool>", plus Diff in Navo's compare (Q31).
    - `git-cli`: Pull and Push per D100g; Commit and Log open the terminal from Settings → Terminal in the repository.
    - `none`: Diff (Navo's compare) and "Choose a Git tool…".
  - Launching:
    - Rust builds the argument list; Navo never goes through `cmd /c` or another shell.
    - The tool starts detached, with stdio null and `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` for console programs.
    - TortoiseGitProc gets `raw_arg` so `/path:"C:\a b"` reaches it as written; a trailing backslash on the path is dropped first.
    - A URL-protocol tool goes through the existing `ShellExecuteExW` launch worker.
    - Navo waits for the tool process on a worker and rescans when it exits.
- D100i — **Diff without a tool.** There is no compare view yet (Part U is blocked). When it lands, Diff builds two Locations, `navo_fs::git::location(repo, "HEAD" or the commit that last touched the file, inner)` and the plain local Location, and dispatches the command `compare.open-file-pair { left, right }`. Until Part U defines that command, Diff runs the user's Git tool (diff), and with no tool the item is hidden. `GitHooks::open_diff(repo, path)` is the port; a TODO there names the command.
- D100k — **Subtree badges (Eru, Q29, 2026-11-02; overrides the one-level rule of D100b).** Every folder inside a repository gets a badge, not only the root. A folder shows the worst state of anything below it (conflict over modified over clean); the root also carries ahead and behind. One cached `git status` per repository; the status model aggregates the changed paths into a per-directory map in memory, never one Git call per folder. Existing caps stay; above them subfolders show `unknown` and the root still shows. A clean subfolder shows clean, or nothing if the design pick prefers quiet clean folders. GS1 holds the aggregation and a nested-changes test.
- D100l — **Status goes through the engine choice (Eru's question, 2026-11-02).** The status reader is a `GitStatus` trait with two engines, picked by `navo-git` `choose.rs` and `[git] engine` (`auto`, `gix`, `git`; GS6). With `auto`: `git.exe` for worktree status when installed (gix 0.89 is built without its `status` feature, and G5 routed worktree status to git); with no `git.exe`, gix status. The `gix` `status` feature is turned on (same pinned version) only if GS5's short measurement on a large repository (status time and peak memory against `git.exe`) shows it is good enough; otherwise the no-git badge stays `Unknown(NoGit)`. A repository with filters or `core.fsmonitor` is `unknown` under gix and never runs repository config (D100e). GS5 extends the G5 oracle test: gix status against `git status` on the fixture repos, same counts and conflict set. The GS5 report ends with one line for Eru: the measured time and memory, and whether the feature went on. GS1 (model, engine-neutral) and GS2 (process) do not change; GS4 implements the CLI side of the trait.
- D100m — **The look (Eru's pick, 2026-11-02).** Direction A of `design/prototypes/git-badges/index.html`: one coloured Lucide glyph in the gutter (state by shape and colour), `N↑ M↓` after the name, the Navo popup menu with a head block (repo, branch, state, fetched time), the footer "via <tool>" with the rebindable Alt+G hint. Clean subfolders use B's quiet dot (`.qdot`, 4 px, `--line-strong`), so an empty gutter means "no Git"; a clean repository root shows the check. No tally strip (C) and no count pill (D). Fade-only motion; icons centred on the first text line.
- D100j — **Look and keys.**
  - The badge and the menu come from designer round R13 (three directions; the owner picks).
  - Theme tokens only; Lucide icons registered in `NavoIcons`; the icon centred on the text line (D86).
  - The gutter is one icon cell wide and shows only when the listing holds a repository; the header reserves the same width.
  - Fade only, about 80 ms, none with reduced motion.
  - Alt+G on a repository row opens the menu (Q32); the menu keys are those of `popup_menu`.
  - Ctrl+K commands: `git.menu`, `git.commit`, `git.pull`, `git.push`, `git.log`, `git.diff`. They are hidden when the cursor row has no badge.

### Data kept

Status lives in memory only; nothing goes to `navo.db` or the cache. Settings live in `navo.toml` `[git]`: `badges`, `tool`, `tool-command`, `fetch`, `fetch-minutes` (5–120), `network`. Logs carry the repository path, the state, the duration and the error kind, never file names from the status output.

### Risks and rollback

- R-G1 — **A downloaded repository runs a program when its parent folder is listed** (filter drivers, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.sshCommand` in `.git/config`). Prevented by D100e. GS3 and GS4 test it with marker-file fixtures, and Sol reviews GS3, GS4 and GS8.
- R-G2 — **Navo changes a repository the user did not ask to change.** Prevented by D100g. GS4 proves that `.git/index` keeps its bytes and modified time after a status. GT4 proves that a refused Pull leaves the worktree hash unchanged and that Push moves one branch only.
- R-G3 — **Process storms or UI stalls in folders with many repositories.** Prevented by the D100f limits. The UI reads an `Arc` snapshot and never waits. The GR1 VM check measures a folder of 64 repositories.
- R-G4 — **Password pop-ups from a background fetch.** Prevented by the fetch environment in D100g. A fetch that needs credentials fails quietly and backs off.
- R-G5 — **Git processes left running on Windows.** The real `git.exe` is spawned, never the shim, and every child is killed on cancel and on stop. GR1 checks this on the VM.
- Rollback: `[git] badges = false` stops all scanning at once; reverting the squash commit removes the feature. Nothing is stored, so nothing needs migrating.

Order:

- Before D100 is confirmed: GT0 and GU0 run now, and so does GT1 (a move with no new behaviour).
- After D100: GS1, GS3, GS6 and GH1 start in parallel; they share no file.
- Status chain: GS1 + GS2 (after J1b) + GS3 → GS4 → GS5.
- Scan and fetch: GS1 + GS6 → GS7; GS2 + GS3 → GS8; GS7 + GS8 → GS9.
- OR1 wiring and screens: GS4 + GS7 + GS9 + GH1 → GH2. GH1 → GU1 → GU2 (after the pick). GS6 + GH1 → GU5. OR1 ships after GR1 and GD1.
- OR2: GT0 + GT1 + GS6 → GT2 → GT3; GS8 → GT4; GH2 + GT3 + GT4 → GH3; GU2 → GU3 → GU4; GU5 + GH3 → GU6; GX1 waits for Aulë.
- Only one `navo-git` atom runs at a time (shared `cli/mod.rs` and `lib.rs`).

#### GT0 — Tool facts

- **Change:** `docs/research/2026-11-git-tools.md` with one row per tool: TortoiseGit, GitHub Desktop, Fork, SourceTree, GitKraken. Columns: install paths and registry keys (per-user and per-machine); how to open a repository; per-action commands (commit, pull, push, log, diff or "check for modifications"); URL protocol; whether the process stays alive while its window is open; licence or terms limits on automation. Each fact carries its source link; any fact without one is marked "unverified".
- **Check:** every cell is filled or marked "unverified"; the TortoiseGit row lists `/command:` names for the five actions.

#### GU0 — Designer round R13

- **Change:** `design/prototypes/git-badges/index.html`, three directions behind one picker with neutral labels. Each shows:
  - the gutter with all seven badges, plus stale and unknown;
  - a listing with and one without repositories (nothing moves);
  - the tooltip;
  - the menu with its head, for TortoiseGit, an open-only tool, the Git command line and None;
  - the Pull confirmation for the Git command line.

  Both themes, 390 px and desktop, theme tokens of the app, Lucide icons only, fade only.
- **Check:** the owner picks a direction; the pick is recorded in this section and D100j.

#### GS1 — Status model and parser

- **Change:** new `navo-git/src/status/mod.rs` (types of D100c, `Badge`, `badge(&RepoStatus) -> Badge`) and `status/parse.rs`. The parser `parse_porcelain_v2(&[u8], capped: bool) -> Result<RepoStatus, GitError>` reads these headers: `# branch.oid`, `# branch.head` (`(detached)`), `# branch.upstream`, `# branch.ab +A -B`. It counts records: `1` and `2` add to `changed`, `u` to `conflicted`, `?` to `untracked`; `!` never counts. It accepts a truncated last record when `capped`. A record it does not know is `GitError::Protocol`. Pure functions, no I/O.
- **Check:** `tests/status_parse.rs` with byte fixtures:
  - clean;
  - staged, unstaged and untracked;
  - conflict;
  - detached;
  - unborn (`branch.oid (initial)`);
  - no upstream;
  - `+3 -2`;
  - a truncated record with and without `capped`;
  - the badge order of D100c, one test per rung.

#### GS2 — Process cancel and output cap

- **Change:** in `navo-git/src/cli/process.rs`, `run_process` gains a `Cancel` (an `Arc<AtomicBool>` newtype, exported) checked in the 5 ms wait loop, plus an optional stdout cap. At the cap, the reader stops, the child is killed and `capped = true` is returned. A cancel returns `GitError::Cancelled`, a new variant. Callers that pass no cancel and no cap behave as today. The capped reader follows J1b's `filtered_stream.rs` kill pattern. If `process.rs` would pass 380 lines, the reader goes to a new `cli/process_cap.rs`.
- **Check:**
  - A helper process that prints forever is cut at a 64 KiB cap with `capped`.
  - A cancel set after 50 ms returns within 100 ms.
  - The existing `cli_read`, `cli_changes` and `cli_filters` tests pass unchanged.
  - VM: the killed child is gone (`tasklist` shows no `git.exe` left from the test).
- **Stop if:** J1b is not on `main`.

#### GS3 — Repository guard

- **Change:** new `navo-git/src/trust.rs`. `Guard::read(git_dir: &Path) -> Result<Guard, GitError>` reads only `config` and `config.worktree` of that Git directory, plus their `include.path` and `includeIf` targets, with `gix-config`. It never reads global or system files. `args()` returns the `-c` pairs of D100e for every filter name found, plus `-c core.fsmonitor=false`. `is_guarded()` and `keys()` report the guard-list keys found. A config that cannot be parsed is guarded, with key `config-unreadable`.
- **Check:** `tests/trust.rs`:
  - an empty config is not guarded and gives only the fsmonitor pair;
  - `filter.evil.clean` gives the four neutralising pairs;
  - `core.sshCommand` in an included file is guarded;
  - a global-only key (from a fake `HOME`) is not seen;
  - a broken config is guarded.

#### GS4 — Status through the CLI

- **Change:** new `navo-git/src/cli/status.rs`, `navo-git/src/status/operation.rs` and the `GitStatus` trait (`fn status(&self, guard: &Guard, cancel: &Cancel) -> Result<RepoStatus, GitError>`), implemented for `CliGit`. It runs the D100d command with the guard args, the 8 MiB cap and a 10 s timeout. When capped, it runs `ls-files -u -z` capped at 1 byte. It fills `operation` from the marker files and `fetched_ms` from `FETCH_HEAD`.
- **Check:** `tests/cli_status.rs` on fixture repositories:
  - clean;
  - modified;
  - only untracked;
  - staged;
  - ahead 2;
  - behind 1 (a local bare origin, a commit there, then fetch);
  - diverged;
  - a merge conflict;
  - a rebase stopped on a conflict;
  - detached;
  - unborn;
  - no upstream;
  - a cap of 1 KiB on 500 untracked files gives `capped` and still finds a conflict;
  - a 1 ms timeout gives `Timeout`;
  - `.git/index` has the same bytes and modified time before and after;
  - `filter.evil.clean` and `core.fsmonitor` pointing at a script that writes a marker file, with `.gitattributes` and a stat-dirty file: no marker after status.
  - Windows only: a repository owned by another SID gives `UnsafeDirectory`.

#### GS5 — gix status trial (D20)

- **Change:** enable the `gix` `status` feature and add `navo-git/src/gix_source/status.rs` (`GixGit: GitStatus`). It runs no filter driver and no fsmonitor, and gets ahead and behind from refs and a merge-base walk.
- **Check:**
  - `tests/gix_status.rs` runs every GS4 fixture through both engines and compares `RepoStatus`.
  - The report gives the exe size change and the time of both engines on the navo repository, 5 runs each.
- **Stop if:** gix 0.89 cannot be set to skip filter drivers and fsmonitor, or results still differ after one fix round. Report; D100d then uses `Unknown(NoGit)` without Git.

#### GS6 — `[git]` settings

- **Change:** new `navo-store/src/settings/git.rs` with `GitSettings { badges: bool, tool: String, tool_command: Option<String>, fetch: bool, fetch_minutes: u16, network: bool }`. Defaults: `true`, `"auto"`, `None`, `false`, `15`, `false`. `fetch-minutes` is clamped to 5–120 on load. One field in `Settings` (`model.rs`) and the default snapshot. The engine key waits for QA2.
- **Check:** settings tests: defaults; `fetch-minutes = 1` loads as 5; the snapshot diff shows only the new table.

#### GS7 — Scan service

- **Change:** new `navo-app/src/git/scan.rs` and `git/cache.rs`, with ports `StatusPort` (`status(repo, &Cancel) -> Result<RepoStatus, GitError>`) and `Probe` (`git_dir(&Path) -> Option<PathBuf>`, `is_network(&Location) -> bool`).
  - `GitScan::start(ports, limits, notify: Box<dyn Fn(u64) + Send>)` owns one thread and a bounded queue (256).
  - `observe(folder, generation, names)` probes per D100b and D100f and publishes `Arc<FolderBadges>` with a `revision`: cached states first (stale), then fresh ones.
  - `forget(folder)` cancels that folder's work. `rescan(folder)` queues it again. `stop()` cancels everything and joins within 2 s.
  - At most 2 status calls run at once.
- **Check:** tests with fake ports:
  - 3,000 folder rows probe 2,000;
  - a slow probe stops at 500 ms;
  - 100 repositories scan 64, the first ones first;
  - never more than 2 in flight;
  - `forget` sets the cancel of running calls;
  - a timeout gives `Unknown(TimedOut)` and no retry until `rescan`;
  - the 257th repository evicts the least recently used one;
  - an older generation's result is dropped;
  - a network folder is skipped until the setting is on;
  - `stop` returns within 2 s with work queued;
  - the cache capacity is at most 256.

#### GS8 — Fetch

- **Change:** new `navo-git/src/cli/remote.rs` with `CliGit::fetch(&Guard, &Cancel)`. It uses D100g's command and environment and a 60 s timeout. It refuses `Guarded`, `NoUpstream` and `Detached` with typed errors (new `GitError` variants).
- **Check:** `tests/cli_remote.rs` with a local bare origin:
  - fetch moves `behind` from 0 to 1 and updates `FETCH_HEAD`;
  - a guarded repository is refused before any process starts;
  - an origin that needs credentials (an `http://127.0.0.1:<closed port>` remote) fails within the timeout and leaves no prompt process.

#### GS9 — Fetch timer

- **Change:** new `navo-app/src/git/fetch.rs`. While `[git] fetch` is on, every `fetch-minutes` it fetches each repository currently observed, one at a time. It skips network drives unless `network` is on, and skips guarded repositories and repositories without an upstream. A failure doubles that repository's wait, up to 4 h; a success resets it and queues a rescan. It uses an injected clock.
- **Check:** tests with a fake clock and fakes:
  - nothing runs with the setting off;
  - two repositories fetch one after the other;
  - the back-off reaches 4 h and stops there;
  - a repository no longer observed is not fetched;
  - turning the setting off cancels a running fetch.

#### GH1 — Hooks contract

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/git/mod.rs` and `git/hooks.rs`.
  - `GitHooks: Send + Sync` with `observe(&Location, u64, Vec<String>)`, `forget(&Location)`, `badges(&Location) -> Option<Arc<FolderBadges>>`, `subscribe(Box<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>)`, `rescan(&Location)`, `menu(&Location) -> GitMenu`, `run(&Location, GitAction) -> Result<GitStarted, String>` and `tools() -> Vec<ToolRow>`.
  - `FolderBadges { revision, by_name: HashMap<String, RepoBadge> }`.
  - `RepoBadge { kind: BadgeKind, stale, branch, upstream, ahead, behind, changed, untracked, conflicted, fetched_ms, reason }`.
  - `GitAction { Commit, Pull, Push, Log, Diff, Open, ChooseTool }`.
  - `GitMenu { head, items: Vec<GitMenuItem { action, label, confirm: Option<String> }> }`.
  - `NoGit` returns nothing and refuses with `git-off`.
  - `StartOptions.git: Option<Arc<dyn GitHooks>>`. If `window_view/mod.rs` would pass 400 lines, `StartOptions` moves to its own file first, in this atom.
- **Check:** `NoGit` gives no badges and an empty menu; the crate builds with the field unset.

#### GH2 — Wiring (OR1)

- **Change:** new `navo-app/src/git/mod.rs` and `git/hooks.rs`. When `[git] badges` is on: start `GitScan` with a `StatusPort` over `CliGit` (Git found) or `GixGit` (GS5 adopted), else `Unknown(NoGit)`; start the fetch timer; implement the OR1 half of `GitHooks`. `apply(values)` starts and stops both live. One field in `gui.rs`. In this atom, `menu` and `run` return the `none` menu.
- **Check:** tests with fake ports: badges off starts no thread; toggling on and off keeps no stale subscription; a fake status reaches `badges`.

#### GU1 — Badge store in the window

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/window_view/git_badges.rs`. When a pane's listing loads or changes generation, it calls `observe` with the folder rows' names. When a tab leaves a folder, it calls `forget`. `subscribe` triggers `cx.notify`, and stale revisions are dropped. Also `FileListProps.git: Option<Arc<FolderBadges>>` and one line in `pane.rs` `list_props`. No visual change.
- **Check:** tests with fake hooks: a listing load calls `observe` once per generation; leaving the folder calls `forget`; a new revision reaches the props.

#### GU2 — Badge gutter

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/file_list/git_cell.rs`; `row.rs` puts the cell left of the icon per the pick; `header.rs` reserves the same width when `props.git` has any badge; the picked Lucide icons go into `NavoIcons`. The cell's spoken name is "Git: <state>, <n> ahead, <n> behind" and its tooltip follows D100c. The stale look and the fade follow the pick.
- **Check:**
  - Tests: the gutter is absent with no badge and present with one; header and row widths match (L0 probe at 480, 800 and 1280 px); the spoken names.
  - Gallery at 390 px and desktop, both themes; icons line up with the text in zoomed screenshots.

#### GU5 — Settings → Git (OR1 rows)

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/settings/git_fields.rs` and `settings/git_values.rs` (`values.rs` is at 396 lines). `Section::Git` comes after Search. Rows: "Show Git status in the file list", "Fetch in the background", "Every … minutes" (5–120), "Network drives". The help text says that fetch is the only time Navo writes into a repository on its own.
- **Check:** settings tests: defaults; the minutes row clamps; the minutes row is disabled while fetch is off.

#### GR1 — Review OR1

- **VM checks:**
  - a folder with 3 repositories (clean, modified, ahead) shows the right badges;
  - a folder with 64 repositories scrolls with no frame over 16 ms (`--trace`);
  - the GS4 marker fixture leaves no marker;
  - after leaving the folder, `tasklist` shows no `git.exe`;
  - background fetch on, with a credential-protected remote: no pop-up.

#### GT1 — Registry reader in `navo-shell`

- **Change:** move `terminal/system.rs`'s registry reader to a new `navo-shell/src/registry.rs` as `text(Hive::{CurrentUser, LocalMachine}, key, name) -> Option<String>`. It reads the 64-bit view (`RRF_SUBKEY_WOW6464KEY`) and grows the buffer when `ERROR_MORE_DATA`, up to 32,767 units. The terminal calls it; there is no behaviour change. A Unix stub returns `None`.
- **Check:** terminal tests unchanged; Windows test: `LocalMachine`, `SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion`, `ProductName` is not empty.

#### GT2 — Tool detection

- **Change:** new `navo-app/src/git/tools.rs`. `ToolKind` (D100h list), and `detect(&dyn ToolProbe) -> Vec<FoundTool { kind, path }>` in D100h order over the GT0 paths and keys. `ToolProbe { exists(&Path), registry(Hive, &str, &str), env(&str) }`, with a real one for each OS. `resolve(setting, found) -> Option<FoundTool>` applies `auto`, which never resolves to `git-cli`.
- **Check:** tests with a fake probe: TortoiseGit found through the registry, and through Program Files when the key is missing; `auto` with only Git gives `None`; a set tool that is missing gives `None` with a reason row.

#### GT3 — Tool launch

- **Change:** new `navo-app/src/git/launch.rs` (plan, custom template) and `git/launch_windows.rs`.
  - `plan(tool, action, repo) -> Result<Launch, LaunchError>` follows D100h. `Launch { program, args: Vec<Arg::{Plain, Raw}>, console: bool, url: Option<String> }`.
  - `custom` reuses the terminal's `split` and its `{path}` rules (`split` becomes `pub(crate)`).
  - `start(launch, on_exit: Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>)` spawns detached, waits on a worker and calls `on_exit` when the process ends. A URL goes through the launch worker, with no `on_exit`.
  - A repository path with `"` or a control character is refused.
- **Check:**
  - Tests: TortoiseGit commit on `C:\a b\` gives the raw arg `/path:"C:\a b"`; every action maps per the GT0 table; `custom` with `{path}` inside a shell word is refused (terminal rules).
  - VM: the Commit dialog opens with no console window; closing it triggers `on_exit`.

#### GT4 — Pull and Push

- **Change:** `cli/remote.rs` gains `pull_ff_only(&Cancel)` and `push_upstream(&Cancel)`, D100g's commands with a 120 s timeout and normal config. Push refuses detached HEAD and a missing upstream. A Pull that is not a fast-forward returns `NotFastForward`.
- **Check:** `tests/cli_remote.rs`:
  - a fast-forward Pull moves HEAD;
  - a diverged Pull returns `NotFastForward` with the worktree's file hashes unchanged;
  - Push sends only the current branch: another local branch with new commits stays unpushed on the bare origin;
  - Push with no upstream is refused before any process starts.

#### GH3 — Wiring (OR2)

- **Change:** `git/hooks.rs` implements `menu`, `run` and `tools`.
  - Menu items follow D100h for the resolved tool. Pull and Push with `git-cli` carry `confirm` text: branch, remote, commit count and guarded keys.
  - `run` starts the launch or the CLI action on a worker and queues a rescan when it ends.
  - Commit and Log with `git-cli` open the terminal through the existing terminal hooks.
- **Check:** tests with fakes: each tool gives the D100h items; `none` gives Diff (only once QA1 is met) and "Choose a Git tool…"; Pull's confirmation names the branch and the count.

#### GU3 — Badge menu

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/git/menu.rs`. A click on the badge emits `FileListAction::GitMenu(row)`; `git_badges.rs` opens a `popup_menu` with `GitMenu` per the pick. An item with `confirm` asks first through `confirm_dialog`. `run` goes off the UI thread, and the result shows in the pane's status line ("Pushed main to origin", "Pull needs a merge; use your Git tool").
- **Check:** tests: a click opens the menu for that row; Esc closes it and gives focus back to the list; a confirmed Pull calls `run` once; a cancelled one does not. Gallery page `git-menu`, both themes.

#### GU4 — Commands and keys

- **Change:** new `navo-ui/src/commands/git.rs` with `git.menu` (Alt+G, file list context) and `git.commit`, `git.pull`, `git.push`, `git.log`, `git.diff` (no keys). Each is hidden without hooks or when the cursor row has no badge. Strings go in `navo-l10n/locales/en-US/commands.ftl`.
- **Check:** registry and palette snapshots; Alt+G on a repository row opens the menu; Alt+G in the map viewer still changes the grouping.

#### GU6 — Settings → Git (tool rows)

- **Change:** `git_fields.rs` gains "Git tool": a picker from `hooks.tools()` showing each tool's path, plus "Git command line", "Other…" and "None"; Auto shows what it resolved to. It also gains "Command for Other" (a text row with `{path}` and `{action}` help, shown only for Other).
- **Check:** settings tests: Auto shows the resolved tool; Other shows the command row; a bad template shows the terminal parser's message.

#### GX1 — Diff opens Navo's compare

- **Change:** Diff with no tool that can diff calls Aulë's compare entry for `<repo>@HEAD` against the `<repo>` working tree (QA1).
- **Check:** a test with a fake entry: Diff calls it once with both locations; Diff is hidden while the entry is missing.

### Open questions for OR1–OR2

Owner (Eru):

27. **Q27 — Do new, untracked files count as "modified"?** *Recommended: yes; ignored files never count. The tooltip separates "4 changed, 2 new".* **Answered — Eru, 2026-11-02: yes, untracked files count as modified.**
28. **Q28 — Background fetch writes into the repository.** It updates the remote-tracking refs, `FETCH_HEAD` and objects. It would be the only write Navo makes into a repository on its own; D70 said Navo never writes into `.git`. *Recommended: off by default; when on, every 15 minutes, only for repositories shown in a pane, local drives only, never a password prompt, and repositories whose own config can run programs are skipped.* **Answered — Eru: background fetch exists, off by default.**
29. **Q29 — Which folders get a badge?** *Recommended: only repository folders listed in a pane. The folder you are in, and folders inside a repository, show nothing in the beta; a branch chip in the path bar can come later.* **Answered — Eru: badges go on every folder inside a repository, worst state of the subtree (D100k).**
30. **Q30 — With "Git command line" as the tool, may Navo run Pull and Push itself?** *Recommended: yes, after a confirmation that names the branch, the remote and the number of commits. Pull is fast-forward only; Push sends the current branch to its upstream and never forces. Commit and Log open your terminal in the repository. Auto never picks the command line.* **Answered — Eru: yes; Pull and Push run through the git CLI after a confirmation, never a force push.**
31. **Q31 — Tools that can only open a repository** (GitHub Desktop, Fork, and others, per GT0). *Recommended: the menu offers "Open in <tool>" and Diff in Navo's compare. Commit, Pull, Push and Log are not offered for them.* **Answered — Eru: yes; open-only tools show just "Open in <tool>" (no Diff, no other items).**
32. **Q32 — Keys.** *Recommended: Alt+G on a repository row opens the Git menu. Ctrl+K gets Git: Commit, Pull, Push, Log and Diff for the repository under the cursor. There are no direct keys for the actions.* **Answered — Eru: Alt+G, rebindable like every key.**

Orchestrator (Manwë):

- **QM1 — Where does this ship?** D85 put Git in v1.2. *Recommended: OR1 as its own pre-release after B9a, then OR2. The v1.2 Git item keeps history and the remaining commands. The backend atoms touch no B9 file, so they can start as soon as D100 is confirmed.*
- **QM2 — Confirm D100a–D100j**, especially the D100e guard list and the D100g table.

Compare lead (Aulë):

- **QA1 — The entry for Diff.** Which public call opens a pane compare of `<repo>@HEAD` against the `<repo>` working tree (U4, U6, U11), with what signature, and which atom delivers it? *Recommended: one command `compare.open_pair(left: Location, right: Location)` plus U6's `repo@rev` location. GX1 waits for it.*
- **QA2 — Where does the engine setting live?** `[compare] git-engine` (U2), or a shared `[git] engine` shown in Settings → Git and linked from Settings → Compare? *Recommended: a shared `[git] engine` that U2 reads. Status uses the CLI whenever Git is installed, so the setting matters only without Git.*
- **QA3 — May OR atoms add to `navo-git`?** They would add new modules only (`status/`, `trust.rs`, `cli/status.rs`, `cli/remote.rs`, `gix_source/status.rs`) and change `cli/process.rs` (GS2, after J1b). `GitSource` stays as it is. *Recommended: yes, with one `navo-git` atom at a time.*

### Done when

- **OR1:**
  - On Windows, a folder with repositories shows correct badges within 2 s for 10 repositories; a folder without one looks as before.
  - The marker fixtures run nothing, and status leaves `.git/index` untouched.
  - Leaving a folder leaves no `git.exe` running.
  - Background fetch is off by default; when on, it never prompts.
- **OR2:**
  - With TortoiseGit, each menu item opens the right dialog on a path with spaces, with no console window, and the badge refreshes when the dialog closes.
  - With the Git command line, Pull refuses a non-fast-forward and Push sends one branch after a confirmation.
  - With no tool, Diff opens the compare view, or is hidden until QA1 is met.
- **Both:** a review verdict for each half; the full gate once; VM CI green; screenshots at 390 px and desktop in both themes; docs updated.

## Beta exit criteria

All are checked on the release candidate, the last B16 build.

| Criterion | Measured by | Pass |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No open data-safety bugs | `docs/bugs.md` and GitHub issues labelled `data-safety` | zero open |
| No lost or damaged files in daily use | owner's daily use on a Windows PC with retention on | 14 consecutive days, zero incidents |
| Cold start to first usable frame | Windows CI start-up job, median of 10 runs | ≤ 400 ms on 3 consecutive `main` builds |
| Warm start | same job | ≤ 150 ms median |
| RAM, two panes, idle | same job, private bytes after 10 s idle | < 80 MB |
| Soak | 8 h soak run | RSS growth < 10 % after the first hour, handle count flat (± 50), zero panics |
| Retention survives crashes | crash-injection tests for capture, restore and purge on `windows-latest` | all pass |
| Layout | L0 tests at 480, 800 and 1280 px in the gate | pass on Linux and Windows CI |
| Quality | `cargo xtask gate` and Windows CI on the release candidate | green |
| Windows numbers | Record performance zip from a Windows PC | attached to the release; cold start ≤ 400 ms there too |

The start-up and memory CI guards become blocking for the release candidate only; they stay informational for milestone builds (owner, 2026-10-19).

- 2026-11-02 — IX11 found the growth with Linux tests (decision: Tulkas, agreed by Manwë). (A) Deleted index entries were never reclaimed: 250k creates and 250k deletes with 50k live grew slots 50k to 300k, about 64 bytes per churned file, never shrinking (`NameTable::compact` was only called from tests). Fixed as IX11a (5b866632 on `part-quality-ix11`): compaction at a batch boundary when slots are at least 100k and more than twice the live count, and on loading a sparse snapshot; the churn test now asserts bounded slots and bytes over three waves. It holds the index write lock for O(n) once per doubling; to be measured in the VM soak. (B) `Arc::make_mut` in `index_service/batch.rs` deep-copies the whole index when a query holds it during a batch (transient 2x, about 90 MB per 1 M entries): decision with Manwë: ship A, run the VM soak (`scripts/index-churn-soak.ps1`, waves of 250k creates and deletes with 50k live, app attached), then decide B from the numbers. The B test is ignored until then.

- 2026-11-02 — Decisions (Manwë): the git child of GS4 and GS8 inherits Navo's HOME and USERPROFILE unchanged, never a rewritten home, so the guard resolves `~/` includes with the same values git uses; GS4 brief and a test carry it. The GS3 include hole is closed by resolving includes the way git does (git itself lists the effective local and worktree config; the reader cross-checks), not by blocking names such as /proc, because Windows has junctions and \?\ paths; with no git the reader blocks includes that resolve through process-dependent links.

- 2026-11-02 — Sol's block on the secrets paths, two rounds, sorted here. Landed (Boromir and Yavanna): a failed 30 s clipboard clear is retried every 2 s, at most 15 times, and kept for a last attempt at stop and Drop; it is dropped only when the clear worked or a newer copy exists. The memory block of a private write stays locked from creation until the hand-over and is wiped through the lock on any failure; if Windows refuses every re-lock, the block is freed unwiped, because an unlocked movable block has no valid address to write through (documented in `text.rs`; very unlikely). 318 `navo-clipboard` and `navo-shell` Linux tests and clippy pass; the Windows code was never compiled here. Known gaps: no test replaces a secret copy during scheduled retries; the sink call itself can block the worker (stop bounds the caller's wait); `a_panicking_listener_is_counted_and_the_worker_goes_on` is flaky (reads `last_error` before the listener's panic is counted); VM run needed for `windows.rs` tests.

- 2026-11-03 — CB30 review of B9b (Erestor, Sonnet high): APPROVE for the alpha, no path found where a password, sealed secret or note text reaches a log, Debug output, error, row or status line. Findings fixed: row ids were reused after a delete (migration V3 rebuilds `clips` and `saved` with AUTOINCREMENT; Delete all keeps the id high-water marks by staging the fresh database as `clipboard.db.new` before anything is deleted; an upgrade leaves an `upgrade_cleanup` marker so VACUUM and the log truncate retry on every open without blocking the store); deletes of history rows, saved items, secrets and notes answer with the real result and say "not deleted" on failure, with the checkpoint wait (1.5 s) under the hook wait (2 s); a note deleted in the library empties the editor; a pasted password is pushed into the wiped buffer without a plain `String`; quitting saves a note typed in the last half second. Sol (high) blocked the migration twice (cleanup not resumable, Delete all reset ids, a failed cleanup could block opening, seeding after the deletion); the second round was decided by Yavanna without a third look. Known limits: if the last move of `clipboard.db.new` fails after the old files are gone, Delete all reports an error although the data is deleted and the next open moves the file into place; the DELETE statement itself still waits up to 5 s for the write lock; no test covers the Windows migration on NTFS with an antivirus holding the file. Not fixed (logged): a failed note flush at hide gives no message beyond the live "Not saved" line; no test shows Delete all removing saved items, secrets and notes from the files.
- 2026-11-03 — Desktop tester run 1 (Halbarad, Gemini 3.8 Flash high) found a data-safety bug in what shipped on main 2a0f44ba: `clear_if_sequence` never matches after a private write, so the 30 s clear of a copied secret does not fire. Cause: the sequence was read while the clipboard was open and changes when it closes and when the owner window is destroyed. Fix on this branch: `write_private_text` returns the sequence read after both and checks that the private marker is still on the clipboard. Not proven until the four desktop-only tests (`a_private_write_carries_the_three_markers`, `clear_if_sequence_empties_an_unchanged_clipboard`, `paste_returns_focus_and_sends_ctrl_v`, `a_destroyed_window_is_gone`) pass on the desktop; they are on the tester's standing checklist for every clipboard change. The two paste-back tests passed on the desktop in run 1.

- 2026-11-03 — IX8e built (361c03ef and a follow-up) and cross reviewed by Sol (round 1 BLOCK, two points). (1) A second Navo that starts between the process snapshot and the Velopack hand-off is not seen: a snapshot cannot hold exclusivity. Accepted as residual and not fixed: the window is milliseconds, Windows refuses to replace the image of a running exe so the swap fails instead of corrupting, and Velopack has the same property; the VM test with a second `--profile` must still pass (IX8r). (2) A process whose image cannot be read counted only when its name equalled the launching exe; now it also counts when the name starts with `navo` (index service image, renamed copies); new test. No second Sol round; Tulkas sorted it.
- 2026-11-03 — IX8e second Sol look: BLOCK on one point (P2): Velopack restarts Navo in its own working directory, so a relative `--profile`/`--left` value would change meaning after an update. Fixed: a start with any argument that is not a flag and not an absolute path does not apply (it waits for a later plain start); new test. Rest PASS: apply runs before profile choice, instance lock, recovery and tray; no restart loop; the old service image under Program Files is outside the Velopack root, so it never blocks. Windows lint (stubbed C compiler, msvc target) of navo-update, navo-index and navo-app is clean after one nesting fix in the process loop.
- 2026-11-03 — Owner reports on alpha-2026-11-03a (Eru's PC, 2,605,409 objects by Everything). (1) The running service image was the 30 Oct build: Walk→Service only changes routing, and the Settings Install button was enabled only for NotInstalled or Outdated while nothing produced Outdated, so an old installed service could never be updated from the UI; fix in progress as IX8h (install verifies its result, Outdated from the pipe revision, one elevated update per revision). (2) Peak 600–700 MB at 2.6M files, settled 213 MB (linear VM scaling predicts 220 MB settled, 440 MB peak). Measured with a counting allocator (Radagast): a flat build keeps about 71–75 bytes per entry and peaks at 1.55x that from doubling growth; files listed before their parent folder wait in `Pending.blocked` at about 280 bytes each, so 30% such files at 3M add +242 MiB (VmHWM 595 MiB against 245 MiB without them). Atom IX13 (after IX12): slim the blocked state or build in two passes, reserve capacity from the MFT record count, log the peak blocked count. (3) Swings between 10–80 MB and 653 MB at a steady 6% CPU: the code can restart full builds (any journal-apply error drops the index; one unresolved change in a USN batch costs a full MFT rebuild; enumerate deletes the snapshot first) and the service has no log; IX12 adds a rotating service log with a 60 s heartbeat, counters, no rebuild for unresolved changes of unknown files, a 10 minute rebuild limit and the old index serving during a rebuild. (4) Scale gate: the index is also measured at 5 million synthetic files in the VM (full-build peak, settled memory, build time, idle CPU) before and after IX12/IX13 (atom IX14). (5) Network drives: D103; atom IX15 proves by test that nothing starts a full crawl of a network drive, and an architect spec follows IX12 and IX8h.
